A stable ∞ \infty -category for equivariant K ​ K \mathrm{K\!K} -theory
Ulrich Bunke, Alexander Engel, Markus Land

Abstract
For a countable group G G we construct a small, idempotent complete, symmetric monoidal, stable ∞ \infty -category KK sep G \mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G} whose homotopy category recovers the triangulated equivariant Kasparov category of separable G G - C ∗ C^{*} -algebras, and exhibit its universal property. Likewise, we consider an associated presentably symmetric monoidal, stable ∞ \infty -category KK G \mathrm{KK}^{G} which receives a symmetric monoidal functor kk G \mathrm{kk}^{G} from possibly non-separable G G - C ∗ C^{*} -algebras and discuss its universal property. In addition to the symmetric monoidal structures, we construct various change-of-group functors relating these KK-categories for varying G G . We use this to define and establish key properties of a (spectrum valued) equivariant analytic K K -homology theory on proper and locally compact G G -topological spaces, allowing for coefficients in arbitrary G G - C ∗ C^{*} -algebras. Finally, we extend the functor kk G \mathrm{kk}^{G} from G G - C ∗ C^{*} -algebras to G G - C ∗ C^{*} -categories. These constructions are key in a companion paper about a form of equivariant Paschke duality and assembly maps.

1
Introduction and statements

1.1
A small stable
\infty
-category of separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras

Let
G
be a countable group and
A
,
B
be
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras, i.e.
C^{*}
-algebras with an action of
G
by automorphisms. In this situation,
we have the abelian group
K\!K^{G}(A,B)
introduced by Kasparov in his work on the Novikov conjecture
[
Kas88
]
. This group depends contravariantly on the first algebra
A
and covariantly on the second algebra
B
.

The construction of
K\!K^{G}{(A,B)}
can be generalized to graded
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras, to families of
C^{*}
-algebras parametrized by a space as in
[
Kas88
]
, or to the case where
G
is a locally compact groupoid
[
LG99
]
. Though we think that many of our constructions also work in more general situations,
in the present paper we will stick to the classical situation since this is what immediately generalizes to
C^{*}
-categories, and what we need for the applications to Paschke duality and assembly maps in
[
BEL
]
.

If we restrict to separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras, then the Kasparov product

K\!K^{G}(A,B)\otimes K\!K^{G}(B,C)\to K\!K^{G}(A,C)\,,

which has also been introduced in
[
Kas88
]
, can be considered as the composition law of an

{\mathbf{Ab}}
-enriched category
\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
. The objects of this category are the separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras
and the morphism groups are given by

{\mathtt{Hom}}_{\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}}(A,B)\coloneqq K\!K^{G}(A,B)\,.

The category
\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
is additive and the sum is represented by the direct sum of
C^{*}
-algebras.
As observed and exploited in
[
MN06
]
,
\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
has a canonical refinement to a triangulated category.

Often, triangulated categories arise as homotopy categories
of
stable
\infty
-categories
[
Lur
, Sec. 1.1.2]
. The first objective of this paper is to show that the triangulated categories
\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
are no exception to this principle. Standard references for the language of
\infty
-categories in general are
[
Lur09
]
,
[
Cis19
]
. For the definition and properties of stable
\infty
-categories we refer to

[
Lur
, Ch. 1]
.

The first result of this paper is
the construction of a stable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
whose homotopy category is canonically equivalent, as a triangulated category, to
\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
.
This generalizes a construction of
[
LN18
]
from the non-equivariant to the equivariant case.
In the following we provide the precise statement.

Let
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
denote the category of separable, possibly non-unital
C^{*}
-algebras with
G
-action.
We then have a canonical functor

\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}

(1.1)

which is the identity on objects and sends
f\colon A\to B
to the element
[f]
in
K\!K^{G}(A,B)
represented by the Kasparov
(A,B)
-module

(B,f,0)
.

Definition 1.1
.

A morphism
f
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
is called a
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
-equivalence if
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}(f)
is an isomorphism.

We let
W_{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
denote the collection of all
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
-equivalences.
The following definition is the direct generalization of
[
LN18
]
to the equivariant case.

Definition 1.2
.

We define the
\infty
-category

\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\coloneqq{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})[W_{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}^{-1}]

and
let

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}

denote the canonical functor.

Here the
\infty
-category
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})[W_{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}^{-1}]
denotes the Dwyer–Kan localization of

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
at the collection
W_{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
of the
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
-equivalences. Such a Dwyer–Kan localization exists and is characterized by a universal property, see Remark
2.5
for the precise statement. We have the following theorem:

Theorem 1.3
.

1.

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is stable.

2.

We have a canonical factorization

\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ho}}

(1.2)

and
\mathrm{ho}
is an equivalence of triangulated categories.

The proof of Theorem
1.3
is a modification of the argument given for the non-equivariant case in
[
LN18
]
. It will be given in Section
2
.
1
1

1

Note that the link points to the end of the proof.

The specific motivation for the present paper was the need, in the companion paper
[
BEL
]
, to refine the classical equivariant analytic
K
-homology functor with coefficients in a
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
A

X\mapsto K_{A,*}^{G,\mathrm{an}}(X)\coloneqq K\!K_{*}^{G}(C_{0}(X),A)

to a spectrum valued functor. Using Theorem
1.3
we get such a refinement by setting

X\mapsto K_{A}^{G,\mathrm{an}}(X)\coloneqq\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(C_{0}(X),A)\,,

(1.3)

where for the moment
A
must be separable, and is
X
a locally compact and second countable topological
G
-space
so that
C_{0}(X)
is separable, too. Here
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(B,A)
is a short-hand notation for the spectrum
{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}(\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(B),\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A))
.
In Definition
1.14
below we will remove the restrictions on
A
and
X
.

The next theorem lists properties
of the functor
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
and the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}

which reflect well-known properties of Kasparov’s bifunctor
K\!K^{G}
.
We first explain some of the notions appearing in the statement. We consider
a functor from
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras to a stable
\infty
-category. It is called reduced if it sends the zero algebra to a zero object. It is semiexact if it sends every semisplit exact sequence to a fibre sequence, where an exact sequence is semisplit if it admits an equivariant cpc
(completely positive and contractive) split (see also Definition
2.13
.
1
). It is
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable
if it sends morphisms of the form

A\otimes K(H)\to A\otimes K(H^{\prime})

(1.4)

to equivalences, where

H\to H^{\prime}
is an equivariant isometric inclusion of non-zero separable
G
-Hilbert spaces. It is homotopy invariant if it
sends the morphisms of the form

A\to C([0,1])\otimes A

(1.5)

given by the embedding
a\mapsto{1\otimes a}

to equivalences.
Finally, we refer to
[
MN06
, Def. 2.5]
for the notion of admissibility of a diagram
A\colon{\rm Nature}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
.

Theorem 1.4
.

1.

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is reduced.

2.

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is semiexact.

3.

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable.

4.

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is homotopy invariant.

5.

\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
admits countable colimits and is therefore idempotent complete.

6.

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
preserves countable sums.

7.

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
preserves colimits of admissible diagrams
A\colon{\rm Nature}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
.

The proof of this theorem will be given in Section
2
.

The functor (
1.1
) has a characterization by universal properties
for functors to additive categories
[
Hig87
]
,
[
Tho98
]
,
[
Mey00
]
, see Proposition
2.2
.
Our next theorem states that
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
has a similar universal property for functors
from
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
to
objects of the large category
{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{ex}}_{\infty}
of small stable
\infty
-categories and exact functors.

Theorem 1.5
.

The functor
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is initial among functors
from
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
to objects of
{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{ex}}_{\infty}
which are reduced, semiexact and
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable.

The proof of this theorem will be given in Section
2
.
We also have a version for additive targets, see Theorem
2.23
, in which semiexactness is replaced by split-exactness.

Remark 1.6
.

Note that the universal property stated in Theorem
1.5
is different from
the obvious one stating that
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is the initial functor to
\infty
-categories which inverts
{\mathrm{kk}^{G}_{0}}
-equivalences. The latter holds true by definition of
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
as a Dwyer–Kan localization.

Note also that in most references, the universal property is stated for functors which are in addition homotopy invariant. In the unequivariant situation it was first proven by Higson that homotopy invariance follows from split-exactness and
\mathbb{K}
-stability, and in the equivariant situation the same is true, see also Remark
2.3
.
∎

In the following, we consider the minimal and maximal tensor products
\otimes_{\min}
and
\otimes_{\max}
of
C^{*}
-algebras. Both of them equip the category
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
of possibly non-unital
C^{*}
-algebras with
G
-action with a symmetric monoidal structure and preserve separable algebras.

Proposition 1.7

(Proposition
2.20
)
.

The tensor product
\otimes_{?}
for
?
in
\{\min,\max\}
descends to a bi-exact symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
, and
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
refines to a symmetric monoidal functor

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G,\otimes_{?}}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})^{\otimes_{?}}\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G,\otimes_{?}}\,.

Moreover, the tensor structure
\otimes_{?}
on
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
preserves countable colimits in each variable.

1.2
A presentable stable
\infty
-category for
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras

For the purpose of the application in
[
BEL
]
, the restriction of the definition of analytic
K
-homology in (
1.3
)
to separable coefficient algebras
A
is not sufficient.
Therefore we must extend the functor
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
from separable
C^{*}
-algebras to all
C^{*}
-algebras with
G
-action. In order to fix size issues
we choose an increasing sequence of three Grothendieck universes whose elements will be called small, large and very large sets.
All
C^{*}
-algebras are assumed to be small. The category
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
is essentially small, and it follows from the details of the proof of Theorem
1.3
that
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is also essentially small. In contrast,
the category
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
is large, but locally small.

Definition 1.8
.

We define the
\infty
-category

\mathrm{KK}^{G}\coloneqq\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G})

as the
\mathrm{Ind}
-completion of
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
and let

y^{G}\colon\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}

(1.6)

denote the canonical functor.

Remark 1.9
.

If
{\mathcal{C}}
is a small, stable
\infty
-category, then by

[
BGT13
, Prop. 3.2]
an explicit model for the canonical functor

{\mathcal{C}}\to\mathrm{Ind}({\mathcal{C}})
is given by the Yoneda embedding

{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{ex}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}})\,,\quad C\mapsto{\mathtt{map}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}(-,C)\,.

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Ind}({\mathcal{C}})
is compactly generated, presentable and stable. Moreover, the functor
{\mathcal{C}}\to\mathrm{Ind}({\mathcal{C}})^{\omega}
exhibits the full subcategory
\mathrm{Ind}({\mathcal{C}})^{\omega}
of compact objects in
\mathrm{Ind}({\mathcal{C}})
as
the idempotent completion of
{\mathcal{C}}
.

∎

Definition 1.10
.

We define the functor

\mathrm{kk}^{G}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}

as the left Kan extension
of
y^{G}\circ\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
along the inclusion
\mathrm{incl}

as indicated in

\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{incl}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{y^{G}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}\,.}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{G}}
\scriptstyle{\Downarrow}

(1.7)

1.3
Equivariant analytic
K
-homology

Using the Definitions
1.8
and
1.10
we can now extend the definition of the spectrum valued analytic
K
-homology functor (
1.3
)
to all
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras (or even objects of
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
)
A
and locally compact
G
-spaces
X
.

We let
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}
denote the category of locally compact Hausdorff spaces with
G
-action and equivariant, continuous and proper maps. We further consider the category
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}_{+}
with the same objects, but with the larger set of maps

{\mathtt{Hom}}_{G\mathbf{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{prop}}}(X,Y)\coloneqq{\mathtt{Hom}}_{G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}}((X^{+},\infty_{X}),(Y^{+},\infty_{Y}))\,,

where
X^{+}
and
Y^{+}
are the one-point compactifications of
X
and
Y
,
respectively, and a map

f\colon(X^{+},\infty_{X})\to(Y^{+},\infty_{Y})

is a continuous equivariant map
X^{+}\to Y^{+}
with
f(\infty_{X})=\infty_{Y}
.
Equivalently,
a morphism
f\colon X\to Y
in
G\mathbf{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{prop}}
is a partially defined map
X\supseteq U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}Y
on an open subset
U
of
X
with
f
in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}
which corresponds to the map
f^{+}\colon X^{+}\to Y^{+}

such that
f^{+}_{|U}=f
and
f(X^{+}\setminus U)=\{\infty_{Y}\}
.

Two morphisms
X\to Y
in
G\mathbf{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{prop}}

are called properly homotopic if there exists a homotopy

[0,1]\times X\to Y
in
G\mathbf{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{prop}}
between them.

The category
G\mathbf{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{prop}}
is the natural domain of the
functor

C_{0}(-)\colon(G\mathbf{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{prop}})^{\mathrm{op}}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})

which sends a locally compact
G
-space to the
C^{*}
-algebra
of continuous functions vanishing at
\infty
with the induced
G
-action.
More precisely,
C_{0}(X)
is the kernel of the evaluation map

C_{0}(X)\coloneqq{\mathtt{ker}}(C(X^{+})\to{\mathbb{C}})\,,

where the evaluation map takes the value at the point
\infty
.
Since we do not assume that
X
is second countable, the algebra
C_{0}(X)
is in general not separable.

If
Y
is an invariant closed subset of
X
, then we have an exact sequence

0\to C_{0}(X\setminus Y)\to C_{0}(X)\to C_{0}(Y)\to 0

(1.8)

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
.

Definition 1.11
.

We say that
Y
is split-closed in
X
, if the sequence
(
1.8
) is semisplit, i.e. if it admits an equivariant cpc (completely positive and contractive) split.

The following proposition provides sufficient criteria for
Y
being split-closed.

Proposition 1.12

(Proposition
5.1
)
.

The closed invariant subset
Y
of
X
is split-closed in the following cases:

1.

G
acts properly on an invariant neighbourhood of
Y
in
X
and
Y
is second countable.

2.

Y
admits a
G
-invariant tubular neighbourhood.

The notion of split-closedness is introduced since
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
only sends semisplit exact sequences to fibre sequences.

Example 1.13
.

Let
{\rm Nature}\to\beta{\rm Nature}
be the Stone–Čech compactification of the discrete space
{\rm Nature}
and
\partial{\rm Nature}\coloneqq\beta{\rm Nature}\setminus{\rm Nature}
. Then

\partial{\rm Nature}
is not split-closed in
\beta{\rm Nature}
. In fact, it is known that
C_{0}({\rm Nature})
does not even have a closed linear complementary subspace in
C_{0}(\beta{\rm Nature})=C_{b}({\rm Nature})
.
∎

Definition 1.14
.

We define the equivariant analytic
K
-homology functor

K^{G,\mathrm{an}}\colon G\mathbf{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{prop}}\times\mathrm{KK}^{G}\to\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}

by

(X,A)\mapsto K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(X)\coloneqq\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(X),A)\,.

The following theorem states the basic properties of equivariant analytic
K
-homology. All spaces in the statement
belong to
G\mathbf{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{prop}}
, and
A
is in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
or
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
, where in the latter case we drop the functor
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
in order to simplify the notation. In Remark
1.16
below we will explain these statements in more detail.

Theorem 1.15
.

1.

If
X
is second countable and
A
is a
\sigma
-unital
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra, then we have an isomorphism of
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded abelian groups

K_{A,*}^{G,\mathrm{an}}(X)\cong K\!K_{*}^{G}(C_{0}(X),A)\,.

2.

The functor
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}
is homotopy invariant.

3.

If
Y
is a split-closed
G
-invariant subspace of
X
, then we have a fibre sequence

K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(Y)\to K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(X)\to K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(X\setminus Y)\,.

(1.9)

4.

We consider an exact sequence
0\to A\to B\to C\to 0
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
.
If the sequence
is semisplit, or if

X
is properly homotopy equivalent to a finite
G
-CW complex with finite stabilizers, then we have a fibre sequence

K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(X)\to K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{B}(X)\to K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{C}(X)\ .

(1.10)

If
X
is second countable, then

\mathrm{KK}^{G}\ni A\mapsto K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(X)

(1.11)

preserves filtered colimits.

5.

We have

K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}([0,\infty)\times X)\simeq 0\,.

Furthermore, if

(X_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
is a family of second countable spaces and
A
is separable, then we have a canonical equivalence.

K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}\big(\bigsqcup_{n\in{\rm Nature}}X_{n}\big)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\prod_{n\in{\rm Nature}}K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(X_{n})\,.

6.

If
X_{0}\supseteq X_{1}\supseteq\ldots\supseteq X_{n}\supseteq\ldots

is a decreasing sequence of closed invariant subspaces of a second countable space
X_{0}
such that
\bigcap_{n}X_{n}\to{X_{0}}
is split-closed, and
A
is separable, then we have an equivalence

K_{A}^{G,\mathrm{an}}\big(\bigcap_{n\in{\rm Nature}}X_{n}\big)\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{n\in{\rm Nature}}K_{A}^{G,\mathrm{an}}(X_{n})\,.

7.

If
H
is a finite subgroup of
G
, then we have an equivalence

K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(G/H)\simeq K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(A)\rtimes H)\,.

8.

The functor
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}
has a lax symmetric monoidal refinement

{G\mathbf{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{prop}}}^{,\otimes}\times{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\otimes_{?}}\to\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}\otimes}

for
?
in
\{\min,\max\}
.

Remark 1.16
.

In this remark we explain the meaning of the assertions of Theorem
1.15
in greater detail.

The Assertion
1.15
.
1
shows that, under the conditions on
X
and
A
as stated, the functor
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}
is a spectrum valued refinement of the classical equivariant analytic
K
-homology functor.

The homotopy invariance in
X
stated in Assertion
1.15
.
2
first of all means that the functor
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}
sends the projection
[0,1]\times X\to X
to an equivalence. Since we work in the category of proper equivariant maps,
this implies invariance of
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}
under proper equivariant homotopies. Furthermore, for fixed
X
the functor
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{-}(X)
is also homotopy invariant in the algebra variable.

The Assertion
1.15
.
3

implies that
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}
satisfies excision for invariant split-closed decompositions
(Z,Y)
of
X
, i.e., invariant decompositions such that
Y
is split-closed in
X
and
Y\cap Z
is split-closed in
Z
. Furthermore, the fact that
the fibre of
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(Y)\to K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(X)
only depends on the complement
X\setminus Y

is often referred to as the strong excision axiom.
Note that the map
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(X)\to K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(X\setminus Y)
in (
1.9
) is induced by
the partially defined map

X\supseteq X\setminus Y\xrightarrow{{\mathtt{id}}_{X\setminus Y}}X\setminus Y
.

It immediately follows from Definition
1.14
that for fixed
X
,
the functor

\mathrm{KK}^{G}\ni A\mapsto K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(X)\in\mathbf{Sp}

preserves limits. The Assertion
1.15
.
4
states additional exactness properties of
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}(X)
as a functor on
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
rather than
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
.

The Assertion
1.15
.
5

is our expression of local finiteness of
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}
. The second part is often referred to as the cluster axiom.

The Assertion
1.15
.
6
is also called the continuity axiom.

Following
[
LN18
]
, in Assertion
1.15
.
7

we use the spectrum valued
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-algebras

K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}}(-)\coloneqq\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},-)\colon C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{Sp}

(1.12)

which is equivalent to the one constructed in e.g.
[
Joa03
]
,
[
Joa04
]
; see
[
LN18
, Prop. 3.7.1]
. Let
G\mathbf{Orb}
be the orbit category of
G
, i.e. the full subcategory of
G
-sets consisting of transitive
G
-sets, and let
G_{\mathbf{Fin}}\mathbf{Orb}
denote its full subcategory on orbits with finite stabilizers.
Considering
G
-sets as discrete topological spaces, we have an embedding
G_{\mathbf{Fin}}\mathbf{Orb}\to G\mathbf{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{prop}}
and can define a functor

K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}\colon G_{\mathbf{Fin}}\mathbf{Orb}\to G\mathbf{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{prop}}\xrightarrow{K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}}\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}\,.

(1.13)

The Assertion
1.15
.
7
implies that
this functor has the same values as the Davis–Lück functor used in
[
DL98
]
,
[
Joa03
]
,
[
LNS17
]
(for
A={\mathbb{C}}
) and
[
Kra
]
(in general). In
[
BEL
]
, we will upgrade this to an equivalence of functors, see (
A.9
) for a precise statement.
In the Appendix
A
we explain how the functor
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{A}
, which is involved in the definition of the domain of the spectrum valued Baum–Connes assembly map, features in a comparison of assembly maps, see Diagram
. Its construction is one of the motivations for the companion paper
[
BEL
]
.

In Assertion
8
the symmetric monoidal structure on
{G\mathbf{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{prop}}}^{,\otimes}
is given by the cartesian product of the underlying topological spaces.
∎

The functor
K^{G,\mathrm{an}}
will be derived in (
5.6
)
from the more fundamental functor

\mathrm{kk}^{G}C_{0}\coloneqq\mathrm{kk}^{G}\circ C_{0}\colon G\mathbf{LCH}_{+}^{\mathrm{prop}}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}

whose properties will be stated in Theorem
5.2
.

The proof of Theorem
1.15
(and of Theorem
5.2
) will employ almost all of the general results about
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
stated below.
It will be completed in Section
5
.

1.4
The s-finitary extension

We now come back to the properties of the functor
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
from Definition
1.10
.

Definition 1.17
.

A functor
F
defined on
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
is called s-finitary if for every

A
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
the canonical map

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}A}F(A^{\prime})\to F(A)

(1.14)

is an equivalence, where
A^{\prime}
runs through the separable
G
-invariant subalgebras of
A
.

The prefix ‘s’ stands for separable.
In contrast, in the literature a finitary functor is usually required to preserve all filtered colimits.
The following theorem lists the basic properties of
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
.

Theorem 1.18
.

1.

\mathrm{kk}^{G}
is s-finitary.

2.

\mathrm{kk}^{G}
is reduced.

3.

\mathrm{kk}^{G}
is semiexact.

4.

\mathrm{kk}^{G}
is homotopy invariant.

5.

\mathrm{kk}^{G}
is
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable.

The proof of this theorem will be finished in Section
3
.

In order to formulate the universal property of
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
we consider the very large
\infty
-category category
\mathbf{CAT}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{ccpl}\cap\mathrm{ex}}
of cocomplete (with respect to small colimits) stable
\infty
-categories and functors preserving
small colimits. As noted in Remark
1.9
the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
is presentable stable and
therefore belongs to
\mathbf{CAT}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{ccpl}\cap\mathrm{ex}}
.

Theorem 1.19

(Theorem
3.3
)
.

\mathrm{kk}^{G}
is initial among functors from
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
to objects of
\mathbf{CAT}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{ccpl}\cap\mathrm{ex}}

which are s-finitary, reduced,
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable and semiexact.

Assume that
A,B
are in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
. Then on the one hand, we
have the abelian group
K\!K_{0}^{G}(A,B)
defined by Kasparov in
[
Kas88
]
. On the other
hand, we have the abelian group

\pi_{0}\mathrm{KK}^{G}(A,B)
defined by the abstract categorical procedure in Definition
1.8
.
If both
A
and
B
are separable, then these two groups are identified by the morphism
\mathrm{ho}
in (
2.14
).
The next proposition extends this isomorphism to all degrees and from separable to
\sigma
-unital
B
.
2
2

2

Using
[
Ska85
, Sec. 3]
on can remove the assumption that
B
is
\sigma
-unital. For separable
A
the functor
K\!K_{0}(A,-)
is
s
-finitary. Thereby various definitions of the functor coincide.

We let
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\sigma}
be the full subcategory of
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
of
\sigma
-unital
C^{*}
-algebras.

Proposition 1.20

(Proposition
3.5
)
.

For any objects
A
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
and
B
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\sigma})
, the functor
\mathrm{ho}
induces
an isomorphism of
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded abelian groups

\pi_{*}\mathrm{KK}^{G}(A,B)\cong K\!K^{G}_{*}(A,B)\,.

Note that this proposition for separable
B

is a direct consequence of the compatibility of
\mathrm{ho}
with the
triangulated structure stated in Theorem
1.3
.
2
.

The following result extends Proposition
1.7
from the separable to the general case.

Proposition 1.21

(Proposition
3.8
)
.

The symmetric monoidal structure
\otimes_{?}
on
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
for
?
in
\{\min,\max\}
canonically induces a
presentably symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
and
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
refines to a symmetric monoidal functor

\mathrm{kk}^{G,{\otimes}_{?}}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\otimes_{?}}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G,{\otimes}_{?}}\,.

As an immediate consequence of Proposition
1.21

we can define for
?
in
\{\min,\max\}
an internal morphism functor

\mathrm{kk}^{G}_{?}(-,-)\colon(\mathrm{KK}^{G})^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathrm{KK}^{G}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}\,.

(1.15)

It is
characterized by a natural equivalence

\mathrm{KK}^{G}(A,\mathrm{kk}^{G}_{?}(B,C))\simeq\mathrm{KK}^{G}(A\otimes_{?}B,C)
for all
A,B,C
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}

and preserves limits in both arguments.

1.5
Change of groups functors

In the following we consider various change of groups functors.
If
H\to G
is a homomorphism of groups, then we have an obvious restriction functor

\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\,.

If
H
is a subgroup of
G
, then we have an induction functor

\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})

which will be explained in Construction
4.9
.
Finally, we have maximal and reduced crossed product functors

-\rtimes_{\max}G,-\rtimes_{r}G\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\to C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}

whose details will be recalled in
Construction
4.14
.
The following results say that these functors descend to functors (denoted by the same symbols) between the corresponding
stable
\infty
-categories.

Theorem 1.22
.

1.

There exists a factorization

\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Res}_{H}^{G}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{G}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{H}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Res}_{H}^{G}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{H}}

and
\mathrm{Res}_{H}^{G}\colon\mathrm{KK}^{G}\to\mathrm{KK}^{H}
preserves colimits and compact objects.

2.

There exists a factorization

\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{H}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{G}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{H}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}

and
\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}\colon\mathrm{KK}^{H}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}
preserves colimits and compact objects.

3.

There exists a factorization

\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rtimes_{?}G}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{G}}
\textstyle{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{-\rtimes_{?}G}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}}

for
?\in\{r,\max\}

and
-\rtimes_{?}G\colon\mathrm{KK}^{G}\to\mathrm{KK}
preserves colimits and compact objects.

The functor in Assertion
3
induces on mapping spectra a spectrum level version of Kasparov’s descent morphism
[
Kas88
]
.

Since
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is idempotent complete by Theorem
1.4
.
5
, the functor
y^{G}
in (
1.6
) identifies
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
with the full subcategory of
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
of compact objects, see Remark
1.9
.
Hence the assertion that the functors preserve compact objects means that they induce functors between the
separable versions of the respective
\mathrm{KK}
-categories. It then follows from the assertion about preservation of colimits
that the functors appearing in Theorem
1.22
are equivalent to the canonical extensions of their separable versions.
The proof of Theorem
1.22
will be given in Section
4
.

There exists a canonical natural transformation

\iota\colon{\mathtt{id}}\to\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}\circ\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}\,,

(1.16)

of endofunctors of
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
which will be explained in detail after (
4.5
). It looks like the unit of an adjunction, and it indeed becomes the unit of an adjunction after application of
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
(and it is not one before), see (
1.20
) below.
The transformation
\iota
induces the first transformation of functors in

{-}\rtimes_{?}H\to\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}\circ\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{H}(-)\rtimes_{?}H\to\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{H}(-)\rtimes_{?}G\,,

(1.17)

where the second is canonically induced by the inclusion of
H
into
G
, see (
4.13
).

We now assume that
H
is a finite group. If
A
is in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
, then we
consider the homomorphism

\epsilon_{A}\colon A\to\mathrm{Res}_{H}(A)\rtimes H\,,\quad a\mapsto\frac{1}{|H|}\sum_{h\in H}(a,h)\,,

where
\mathrm{Res}_{H}(A)
denotes
A
equipped with the trivial
H
-action and we refer to Construction
4.14
for the notation
(a,h)
for elements in
\mathrm{Res}_{H}(A)\rtimes H
.
The family

\epsilon=(\epsilon_{A})_{A\in C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}}
is a natural transformation

\epsilon\colon{\mathtt{id}}\to\mathrm{Res}_{H}(-)\rtimes H

(1.18)

of endofunctors of
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.

Let
B
be in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. Then we have a canonical homomorphism

\lambda_{B}\colon\mathrm{Res}_{G}(B)\rtimes_{\max}G\to B

of
C^{*}
-algebras which corresponds to the covariant representation

({\mathtt{id}}_{B},\mathrm{triv})
consisting of the identity of
B
and the trivial representation of
G
. The family

\lambda=(\lambda_{B})_{B\in C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}}
is a natural transformation

\lambda\colon\mathrm{Res}_{G}(-)\rtimes_{\max}G\to{\mathtt{id}}

(1.19)

of endofunctors of
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.

The Assertions
1
and
3

of the following theorem
are
\infty
-categorical level versions of Green’s Imprimitivity Theorem
[
Gre78
]
(for
?=\max
),
[
Kas88
]
and the Green–Julg theorem
[
Jul81
]
. Both statements generalize the versions for the triangulated categories stated in
[
MN06
]
.
Assertion
4
is known as the dual Green–Julg theorem.

Theorem 1.23
.

1.

The natural transformation (
1.16
) induces the unit of
an adjunction

\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}:\mathrm{KK}^{H}\rightleftarrows\mathrm{KK}^{G}:\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}\,.

(1.20)

2.

The transformation (
1.17
) naturally induces an equivalence of functors

{-}\rtimes_{?}H\to\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(-)\rtimes_{?}G\colon\mathrm{KK}^{H}\to\mathrm{KK}

for
?
in
\{{r},\max\}
.

3.

If
H
is finite, then the natural transformation
(
1.18
) induces the unit of an adjunction

{\mathrm{Res}_{H}}:\mathrm{KK}\rightleftarrows\mathrm{KK}^{H}:-\rtimes H\,.

4.

The natural transformation (
1.19
)
induces the counit of an adjunction

-\rtimes_{\max}G:\mathrm{KK}^{G}\rightleftarrows\mathrm{KK}:{\mathrm{Res}_{G}}\,.

Since
H
is finite, the crossed product in Assertion
3
need not be decorated. It is simply the algebraic crossed product which happens to be equal to the reduced and maximal one in this case. Note that Assertion
2
in the case
?=\max
is also a consequence of Assertions
1
and

4
.
The proof of this theorem will be completed in Section
4
.

Note that
\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}({\mathbb{C}})\cong C_{0}(G/H)
. Let

r^{G}_{H}\colon\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(G/H),-)\simeq\mathrm{KK}^{H}({\mathbb{C}},\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(-))

(1.21)

denote the equivalence of the mapping spectrum functors given by the adjunction in the Theorem
1.23
.
1
.
Furthermore, let

\mathrm{GJ}^{H}\colon\mathrm{KK}^{H}({\mathbb{C}},\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(-))\simeq\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(-)\rtimes H)

denote the equivalence of the mapping spectrum functors given by the adjunction
in the Theorem
1.23
.
3
.

Corollary 1.24
.

If
H
is a finite subgroup of
G
, then we have an equivalence

\mathrm{GJ}^{H}\circ r^{G}_{H}\colon\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(G/H),-)\to{K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}}(\mathrm{Res}_{H}^{G}(-)\rtimes H)}

(1.22)

of functors from
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
to
\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}
.

By Theorem
1.18
.
3
the functor
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
sends exact sequences in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
admitting equivariant cpc splits to fibre sequences. As an immediate consequence, the functor

\mathrm{KK}^{G}(A,-)\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}})\to\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}

has the same property for every
A
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
. The Theorem
1.27

shows that under certain restrictions on
A
this functor in fact sends all exact sequences to fibre sequences. The conditions are formulated so that we can deduce this exactness using Corollary
1.24
from the fact that the usual
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-algebras in (
1.12
)
sends all exact sequences to fibre sequences.

Recall that a thick subcategory of a stable
\infty
-category
is a full stable subcategory which is closed under
taking retracts. A localizing subcategory of a presentable
stable
\infty
-category is a full stable subcategory which is closed under
all colimits.

Definition 1.25
.

1.

The objects of the thick subcategory of

\mathrm{KK}^{G}
generated by the objects
\mathrm{kk}^{G}(C_{0}(G/H))
for all finite subgroups
H
of
G

are called
G
-proper.

2.

The objects of the localizing subcategory of

\mathrm{KK}^{G}
generated by the
G
-proper objects are called ind-
G
-proper.

Note that
G
-proper objects are ind-
G
-proper.
The following result provides examples of
G
-proper objects in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
.

Proposition 1.26

(Proposition
5.5
)
.

If
X
is in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}
and homotopy equivalent (in
G\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}
) to
a finite
G
-CW complex with (necessarily) finite stabilizers, then

\mathrm{kk}^{G}(C_{0}(X))
is
G
-proper.

Let
P
be an object of
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
.

Theorem 1.27

(Theorem
5.6
)
.

1.

If
P
is ind-
G
-proper, then the functor

\mathrm{KK}^{G}(P,-)\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\to\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}

sends all exact sequences to fibre sequences.

2.

If
P
is
G
-proper, then the functor

\mathrm{KK}^{G}(P,-)\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\to\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}

preserves filtered colimits.

Remark 1.28
.

We define the
thick subcategory of
G
-nuclear objects
of
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
generated by objects of the form
\mathrm{kk}^{G}(\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(A))
for finite subgroups
H
and nuclear
A
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
equipped with the trivial
H
-action. We further define the localizing subcategory of
\mathrm{KK}^{G}

of ind-
G
nuclear objects generated by the
G
-nuclear objects.
Since
{\mathbb{C}}
is nuclear, (ind)-
G
-proper objects are (ind)-
G
-nuclear.
It follows from
[
Ska88
]
that the functor

\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}(A,-):C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathbf{Sp}
sends all exact sequences to fibre sequences provided
A
is nuclear.
Using this fact one can show that the assertions of Theorem
1.27

remain true (with essentially the same proof) with (ind)-
G
-proper replaced by
(ind)-
G
-nuclear.

For instance, if
X
in
\mathbf{LCH}^{\mathrm{prop}}
is separable and
H
is a finite subgroup of
G
, then

\mathrm{kk}^{G}(C_{0}(G/H\times X))
is
G
-nuclear, but not necessarily (ind)-
G
-proper.
∎

1.6
Extensions to
C^{*}
-categories

Again motivated by the applications in
[
BEL
]
, we extend the functor

\mathrm{kk}^{G}
from
C^{*}
-algebras to
C^{*}
-categories with
G
-action.
We refer to the beginning of Section
6
for a more detailed introduction to
C^{*}
-categories.
We have a fully faithful inclusion
of the category
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
of (possibly non-unital)
C^{*}
-algebras into the category
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
of small (possibly non-unital)
C^{*}
-categories which considers a
C^{*}
-algebra as a
C^{*}
-category with a single object.
This inclusion fits into an adjunction

A^{f}:C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\rightleftarrows C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:\mathrm{incl}

(1.23)

first considered by
[
Joa03
]
(see
[
Bun
]
for the non-unital case).

Definition 1.29
.

We define the functor

\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}

as the composition

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})\xrightarrow{A^{f}}{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{kk}^{G}}\mathrm{KK}^{G}\,.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be in
C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.

Definition 1.30
.

We
call
{\mathbf{C}}
separable if
{\mathbf{C}}
has countably many objects and all morphism spaces of
{\mathbf{C}}
are separable.

The following extends
Definition
1.17
from
C^{*}
-algebras to
C^{*}
-categories.

Definition 1.31
.

A functor
F
defined on
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
is called
s-finitary, if for every
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
the canonical map

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}}F({\mathbf{C}}^{\prime})\to F({\mathbf{C}})

(1.24)

is an equivalence, where
{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}
runs through the poset of separable

G
-invariant subcategories of
{\mathbf{C}}
.

As in the case of
C^{*}
-algebras, we added the letter ‘s’ (for separable), since this notion differs from the definition of a finitary functor in
[
BE
, Def. 13.7]
. The latter requires the preservation of all filtered colimits.
As an example, using that
G
is countable one can check that the identity functor on
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
is
s
-finitary.

In the following theorem we list the properties of the functor
\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}
for
C^{*}
-categories. For notions appearing in its statement we refer to the following sources: unitary equivalence:
[
BE
, Def. 3.19]
, weak Morita equivalence:
[
BE
, Def. 18.3]
and Definition
6.12
, exact sequence:
[
Bun
, Def. 8.5]
, flasque: Definition
6.18
and
[
BE
, Def. 11.3]
, maximal crossed product
-\rtimes G
:
[
Bun
, Def. 5.9]
, reduced crossed product
-\rtimes_{r}G
:
[
BE
, Thm. 12.1]

and relative Morita equivalence:
[
BE
, Def. 17.1]
.

Theorem 1.32
.

1.

The functor
\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}
is s-finitary.

2.

The functor
\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}
sends unitary equivalences
to
equivalences.

3.

The functor
\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}
sends weak Morita equivalences to equivalences.

4.

We have an equivalence

\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}(-\rtimes_{{?}}G)\simeq(-\rtimes_{{?}}G)\circ\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}

of functors

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})\to\mathrm{KK}
for
?\in\{r,\max\}
.

5.

If
P
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
is ind-
G
-proper, then the functor
\mathrm{KK}^{G}(P,\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}(-))

sends all exact sequences in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
to fibre sequences.

6.

If
P
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
is
G
-proper, then

\mathrm{KK}^{G}(P,\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}(-))
preserves filtered colimits.

7.

If
P
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
is ind-
G
-proper, then
\mathrm{KK}^{G}(P,\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}(-))
annihilates flasques in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})
.

8.

If
P
in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
is ind-
G
-proper, then
\mathrm{KK}^{G}(P,\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}(-))

sends relative Morita equivalences in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
to
equivalences.

The proof of Theorem
1.32
will be given in Section
6
.

Remark 1.33
.

Using Remark
1.28
in Assertions

5

we could replace the conditions (ind)-
G
-proper by (ind)-
G
-nuclear.

Remark 1.34
.

Let us consider here the case of the trivial group.
In
[
BE
, Def. 13.4]
we introduced the notion of a homological functor
\mathrm{Hg}\colon C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{M}
. By definition, it is a functor to a stable
\infty
-category
\mathbf{M}

which sends unitary equivalences of
C^{*}
-categories to equivalences
and
exact sequences of
C^{*}
-categories to fibre sequences. If
\mathrm{Hg}
in addition preserves filtered colimits, then it is called finitary.

If
P
in
\mathrm{KK}
is ind-proper (or more generally ind-nuclear), then it follows from
Theorem
1.32
(and Remark
1.33
)
that
\mathrm{KK}(P,\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}(-))\colon C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathbf{Sp}
is a homological functor.
If
P
is proper (or more generally nuclear), then
this functor is also finitary.

As an example, if
X
is locally compact and homotopy equivalent to a finite CW-complex, then

P\coloneqq\mathrm{kk}(C_{0}(X))
is proper by Proposition
1.26
, and therefore

\mathrm{KK}(C_{0}(X),\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}(-))
is a homological functor.
More generally, if
A
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
is such that
\mathrm{kk}(A)
is nuclear (e.g. if
A
is
\mathrm{kk}^{G}_{0}
-equivalent to a separable nuclear
C^{*}
-algebra), then

\mathrm{KK}(A,\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}(-))
is a homological functor.

The functor
\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}
itself is not a
\mathrm{KK}
-valued homological functor since it does not send all exact sequences to fibre sequences.
∎

The minimal and maximal tensor products for
C^{*}
-algebras can naturally be extended to
C^{*}
-categories, see
[
Del12
]
for
\otimes_{\max}
, and
[
AV20
]
for both. In Section
7
we will give a comprehensive treatment of both cases. The definition of the maximal tensor product in terms of its universal property is stated in Definition
7.2
, while the analoguous definition of the minimal tensor product is given in Definition
7.6
.

Theorem 1.35
.

The functor
\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}
refines to a symmetric monoidal functor

\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G,{\otimes}_{?}}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\otimes_{?}}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G,{\otimes}_{?}}

for
?
in
\{\min,\max\}
.

The proof of this theorem will be given in Section
7
.

2
A stable
\bm{\infty}
-category of separable
\bm{G}
-
\bm{C^{*}}
-algebras

Let
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\sigma}
denote the full subcategories of
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
of separable and of
\sigma
-unital
C^{*}
-algebras, respectively. In the present paper we work with the bivariant
G
-equivariant Kasparov
K\!K
-theory functor
[
Kas88
]

K\!K_{*}^{G}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\sigma})^{\mathrm{op}}\times{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\sigma})\to{\mathbf{Ab}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}}\,,

where
{\mathbf{Ab}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}}
denotes the category of
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded abelian groups.
Further good references for this functor and its properties are

[
Mey00
]
and
[
Bla98
]
.

We will use the symbol

K\!K^{G}\coloneqq K\!K_{0}^{G}
for the
{\mathbf{Ab}}
-valued functor obtained from
K\!K^{G}_{*}
by extracting the degree-zero component. Using the Kasparov intersection product in order to define the composition we construct the
{\mathbf{Ab}}
-enriched category
\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
and the functor

\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}

(appearing in (
1.1
))
as explained in the introduction.
The functor
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
is reduced, homotopy invariant and
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable since it inherits these properties from

K\!K^{G}
.

Recall the Definition
1.1
of a
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
-equivalence.
The functor
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
can be characterized by the following universal property:

Proposition 2.1
.

The functor
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}

exhibits the target as localisation of
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})

at the set of
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
-equivalences in the sense of ordinary categories.

Proof.

Let

F\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to{\mathcal{D}}

be any functor
to an ordinary category
{\mathcal{D}}
which sends
\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
-equivalences to isomorphisms in
{\mathcal{D}}
. We must show that there exists a unique factorisation through
\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
, indicated by the dashed arrow in the following diagram

\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{D}}\,.}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bar{F}}

(2.1)

Since
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
is the identity on objects we are forced to define
\bar{F}(A)\coloneqq F(A)
for all objects
A
of
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
.

For the discussion of morphisms we use
the Cuntz picture of
K\!K^{G}
due to
[
Mey00
, Sec. 6]
.
Let

K\coloneqq K(\ell^{2}\otimes L^{2}(G))

(2.2)

denote the algebra of compact operators on the
G
-Hilbert space
\ell^{2}\otimes L^{2}(G)
, where
G
acts on
L^{2}(G)
by the left-regular representation, and by conjugation on the operators. We furthermore let

q_{s}(A):={\mathtt{ker}}(A\otimes K\sqcup A\otimes K\to A\otimes K)

be the kernel of the fold map. Note that the coproduct in
C^{*}
-algebras is realized by the free product. By
[
Mey00
, Thm. 6.5]
we have a bijection

K\!K^{G}(A,B)\cong[q_{s}(A)\otimes K,q_{s}(B)\otimes K]\,,

(2.3)

where the notation
[-,-]
stands for norm-continuous homotopy classes of morphisms in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
.
Moreover, the composition in
\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
corresponds to the composition of homotopy classes.

We consider the
G
-Hilbert space

H^{\prime}\coloneqq{\mathbb{C}}\oplus\ell^{2}\otimes L^{2}(G)
and the associated
G
-
C^{*}
-algebra
K^{\prime}\coloneqq K(H^{\prime})
of compact operators. The equivariant inclusion
{\mathbb{C}}\to H^{\prime}
induces an inclusion
d:{\mathbb{C}}\to K^{\prime}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
, where
{\mathbb{C}}
has the trivial
G
-action. We also have a canonical inclusion
e\colon K\to K^{\prime}
.

Let
\pi_{A}\colon q_{s}(A)\to A\otimes K
be the restriction of
{\mathtt{id}}_{A\otimes K}\sqcup 0
to
q_{s}(A)
. It is shown in
[
Mey00
, Sec. 6]
that
\pi_{A}
is a
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
-equivalence.
For every
A
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
we have a zig-zag

q_{s}(A)\otimes K\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{A}\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{K}}}{{\to}}{A}\otimes K\otimes K\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathtt{id}}_{A}\otimes e\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{K}}}{{\to}}A\otimes K^{\prime}\otimes K\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathtt{id}}_{A}\otimes d\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{K}}}{{\leftarrow}}A\otimes K\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathtt{id}}_{A}\otimes e}}{{\to}}A\otimes K^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathtt{id}}_{A}\otimes d\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{K}}}{{\leftarrow}}A

(2.4)

which is natural in
A
. By the

\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stability of
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
all these maps are
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
-equivalences.
We now consider the diagram

\displaystyle\mathclap{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 219.35869pt\raise-43.02924pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\eqref{qfrojqoiefjqewfefefqfqewfqewf}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 278.12846pt\raise-55.29033pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\cong}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 330.67946pt\raise-40.91226pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 374.24977pt\raise-65.3684pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}

The left (or right) vertical isomorphism is induced by applying
F
(or
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
) to the zig-zag (
2.4
) using the fact that
F
(or
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
) sends
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
-equivalences to isomorphisms. If
\bar{F}
exists, then the outer square
and the two triangles involving dashed and bold arrows
commute.

Using the lower right triangle we see that the arrow marked by
!
is surjective.
This shows that the arrow marked by
!!
is uniquely determined if it exists.

Since
F
inverts
\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
-equivalences, it also sends homotopic maps to equal maps.
Consequently, the arrow
F
marked by
!!!

factorizes uniquely through the dotted arrow

F^{\prime}
as indicated.
We are therefore forced to define

\bar{F}_{A,B}:K\!K^{G}(A,B)\to{\mathtt{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}(A,B)

as the composition
going clockwise from the upper right-corner to the upper left corner.
The whole diagram is bi-natural in
A
(contravariant) and
B
(covariant).
This implies that
the family
(\bar{F}_{A,B})_{A,B\in{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})}

provides the action of the desired functor
\bar{F}
on morphisms. The triangle (
2.1
) then commutes by construction.
∎

The following universal property of
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
(which differes from the one in Proposition
2.1
) was
shown in
[
Tho98
, Thm. 2.2]
, see also

[
Mey00
, Thm. 6.6]
. A
functor from
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
to an additive category is called split exact if it preserves split-exact sequences, see also Definition
2.13
.
2
.

Proposition 2.2
.

The functor

\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}:{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\longrightarrow\mathrm{KK}^{G}_{0}

is initial among all functors to an additive
1
-category which are reduced,
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable and split exact.

Remark 2.3
.

The statement of Meyer requires additivity and homotopy invariance of the functors. But note that the condition of being split exact and additive are equivalent to the condition of being split exact and reduced. Furthermore, we use that
split-exactness and
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stability together imply homotopy invariance (see
[
Hig88
]
for the non-equivariant case). We refer to
[
CMR07
, Thm. 3.35]
for an argument which applies verbatim to the equivariant case.

∎

Corollary 2.4
.

A functor from
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
to an additive

\infty
-category which is reduced,
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable and split exact sends
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
-equivalences to equivalences.

Proof.

Let
F:{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to{\mathcal{D}}
be a functor as in the statement of the corollary.
Since
\mathrm{ho}({\mathcal{D}})
is an additive
1
-category we can apply Proposition
2.2
to the composition

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F}}{{\to}}{\mathcal{D}}\to\mathrm{ho}({\mathcal{D}})
in order to conclude that it factorizes over
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
. In particular, it sends
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
-equivalences to isomorphisms.
Since the canonical functor
{\mathcal{D}}\to\mathrm{ho}({\mathcal{D}})
detects equivalences we conclude that

F
sends
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
-equivalences to equivalences.
∎

Remark 2.5
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a small
\infty
-category and
W
be a set of morphisms in
{\mathcal{C}}
. Then we can form
the Dwyer–Kan localization
[
Lur
, Def. 1.3.4.1, Rem. 1.3.4.2]

\ell\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{C}}[W^{-1}]\,.

(2.12)

It is characterized by the universal property that for any
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{D}}
the restriction along
\ell
induces an equivalence

{\mathbf{Fun}}({\mathcal{C}}[W^{-1}],{\mathcal{D}})\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{W}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})\,,

(2.13)

where
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{W}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})
is the full subcategory of

{\mathbf{Fun}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})
of functors which send morphisms in
W
to equivalences.
∎

Note that we consider ordinary categories as
\infty
-categories using the nerve, but we will not write the nerve explicitly.
With these conventions we can consider
the functor

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}:{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}

introduced in Definition
1.2
.

We now start with the proof of Theorem
1.3
following the lines of
[
LN18
]
using
[
Mey00
]
and
[
Uuy13
]
.
First of all it follows from the universal property of the Dwyer–Kan localization that there exists a factorization of
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
over a functor
\mathrm{ho}
as indicated by the following commuting triangle:

\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}\,.}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ho}}

(2.14)

This is the commutative triangle in (
1.2
).

In order to show that
{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}
is stable we need the explicit model for the suspension functor

S\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\,,\quad A\mapsto C_{0}((0,1))\otimes A\,.

(2.15)

Lemma 2.6
.

1.

The functor
S
uniquely descends to an equivalence
S_{0}
of additive categories such that

\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S_{0}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}}

(2.16)

commutes.

2.

The functor

S
essentially uniquely descends to a functor (also denoted by)
S
completing the square

\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}

(2.17)

Proof.

In order to obtain
S_{0}
,
we apply Proposition
2.2
to the composition

\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}\circ S\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}\,.

It straightforward to check that the functor
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}\circ S
is reduced,
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable and split exact, using that
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
has these properties.
The functor
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}\circ S

therefore factorizes over an additive endofunctor

S_{0}
as required. It then follows from Bott periodicity that
S_{0}
is an equivalence of categories.

Since by definition
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
detects
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
-equivalences,
we conclude from Assertion
1
that
S
in (
2.15
) preserves
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
-equivalences. Applying the universal property of
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
being a Dwyer-Kan localization (see Definition
1.2
) we obtain the essentially unique factoriaztion of
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\circ S
through
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
as asserted in Assertion
2
.
∎

As shown in
[
Kas88
]
, for every semisplit exact sequence

0\to I\to A\to B\to 0

(2.18)

we have a boundary operator

\partial
in
K\!K^{G}(S(B),I)
such that
the sequence

K\!K^{G}(D,S(A))\to K\!K^{G}(D,S(B))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial}}{{\to}}K\!K^{G}(D,I)\to K\!K^{G}(D,A)\to K\!K^{G}(D,B)

(2.19)

is exact for every
D
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
.
The boundary operator is natural in the sequence in the following sense.
If

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{I\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{I^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

is a morphism to a second sequence of this kind, then the square

\textstyle{K\!K^{G}(D,S(B))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\textstyle{K\!K^{G}(D,I)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\!K^{G}(D,S(B^{\prime}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\prime}}
\textstyle{K\!K^{G}(D,I^{\prime})}

commutes.

The main problem in the proof of Theorem
1.3
is to control finite limits in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
.
To this end we calculate the Dwyer-Kan localization in Definition
1.2
using a category of fibrant objects structure on
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
.
We now recall the definition of a category of fibrant objects in the form used in
[
Uuy13
, Def. 1.1]
.
Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a category with a terminal object and
F
and
W
be a collection of morphisms in
{\mathcal{C}}
.

Definition 2.7
.

The triple
({\mathcal{C}},F,W)
is a category of fibrant objects if
the following conditions hold.

1.

F0:
F
is closed under compositions.

2.

F1:
F
contains all isomorphisms.

3.

F2: Pull-backs of morphisms in
F
exist and belong again to
F
.

4.

F3: For every
C
in
{\mathcal{C}}
the morphism
C\to*
belongs to
F
.

5.

W1:
W
contains all isomorphisms.

6.

W2:
W
has the
2
-out-of-
3
-property.

7.

FW1:
W\cap F
is stable under forming pull-backs.

8.

FW2: For every
C
in
{\mathcal{C}}
the morphism

C\to C\times C
has a factorization

C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle w}}{{\to}}C^{I}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}C\times C
, where
w
is in
W
and
f
is in
F
.

Note that the product in the formulation of Condition FW2 exists by a combination of Conditions F3 and F2.

Remark 2.8
.

The object
C^{I}
appearing in FW2 is called a path object. From the existence of a path object
C^{I}

one can construct a factorisation of an arbitrary morphism
\phi\colon A\to C
in
{\mathcal{C}}
as a weak equivalence followed by a fibration as follows. We choose a factorization
C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle w}}{{\to}}C^{I}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}C\times C
as in
FW2
. Then we
consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\times_{C}C^{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\times C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C\times C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathtt{pr}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi\times{\mathtt{id}}}

(2.20)

where the square is a pull-back. It exists by
F2
which also implies that
f^{\prime}
is a fibration. Using
F3
and
F2
one checks that the projection
{\mathtt{pr}}
is also a fibration. We conclude by
F0
that
{\mathtt{pr}}\circ f^{\prime}
is a fibration.
Furthermore, the map
u

is given by
A\cong A\times_{C}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle A\times w}}{{\to}}A\times_{C}C^{I}
and therefore a weak equivalence by
FW1
and
F3
(applied to
A\to*
).
Consequently

A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u}}{{\longrightarrow}}A\times_{C}C^{I}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathtt{pr}}\circ f^{\prime}}}{{\longrightarrow}}C

(2.21)

is the desired factorization of
\phi
as a composition of a weak equivalence and a fibration.
∎

Remark 2.9
.

The category
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
admits a canonical path object
C\mapsto C([0,1],C)
. The factorization

C\longrightarrow C([0,1],C)\longrightarrow C\times C

required in
FW2
is given by the inclusion of constant maps and the evaluation at
0
and
1
.
The first map is a homotopy equivalence. Furthermore,
the map
C^{I}\to C\times C
is surjective and admits an equivariant contractive completely positive split. For example, one can take the split

(c_{0},c_{1})\mapsto(t\mapsto(1-t)c_{0}+tc_{1})\,.

(2.22)

Applying the general factorization (
2.21
) to a morphism
A\to C
, one obtains the usual cone sequence

0\longrightarrow C(\phi)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Cyl}(\phi)\longrightarrow C\to 0\,.

Explicitly,

\mathrm{Cyl}(\phi)\coloneqq\{(a,c)\in A\oplus C([0,1],C)\>|\>c(0)=\phi(a)\}\,,

(2.23)

the homomorphism
\mathrm{Cyl}(\phi)\to C
is given by
(a,c)\mapsto c(1)
, and

C(\phi):=\{(a,c)\in\mathrm{Cyl}(\phi)\>|\>c(1)=0\}

with the induced
G
-actions.

∎

A morphism
\phi\colon A\to C
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
is called a semisplit surjection if it is a surjection and
admits an equivariant cpc split.
We let
\mathrm{SS}
denote the set of semisplit surjections. Furthermore recall that
W_{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
is the set of
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
-equivalences.

Proposition 2.10
.

The triple
({\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}),\mathrm{SS},W_{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}})
is a category of fibrant objects.

Proof.

The axioms
F0
,
F1
,
F3
,
W1
, and
W2
are obvious.

We show
F2
. We consider a pullback

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D}

(2.24)

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
such that
\phi
is in
\mathrm{SS}
with equivariant cpc split
s:D\to C
. The square of the underlying Banach spaces is a pullback square of Banach spaces. Thus the equivariant contractive split
s
of
\phi
induces an equivariant contractive split
t:B\to A
. We now use the fact that an element in
A
is positive if its images in
C
and
B
are positive. The same is true for the extension of the square to finite matrices. Using that
s
is completely positive we can now conclude that
t
is completely positive as well. Hence
\psi
is in
\mathrm{SS}
, too.

To show
FW2
, we make use of the cylinder discussed in Remark
2.9
. The map
C\to C^{I}
is a homotopy equivalence, hence in particular a
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
-equivalence. Furthermore, as explained in Remark
2.9
the projection
C^{I}\to C\times C
has an equivariant contractive completely positive split and therefore belongs to
\mathrm{SS}
.

In order to prepare the proof of
FW1
we consider an exact sequence admitting an equivariant cpc split.

0\longrightarrow I\longrightarrow A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\psi}}{{\longrightarrow}}B\longrightarrow 0\,.

(2.25)

As explained above, then we have the boundary operator

\partial
in
K\!K^{G}(S(B),I)
such that
the sequence

K\!K^{G}(D,S(A))\to K\!K^{G}(D,S(B))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial}}{{\to}}K\!K^{G}(D,I)\to K\!K^{G}(D,A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\psi_{*}}}{{\to}}K\!K^{G}(D,B)

(2.26)

is exact for every
D
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
.
By the Yoneda Lemma, the morphism
\psi
is a
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
-equivalence if and only if
\psi_{*}
is an isomorphism for every
D
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
.
We conclude from the exactness of (
2.26
) that this is the case if and only if
K\!K^{G}(D,I)=0
for every
D
.

We now consider a pull-back square (
2.24
) in

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
such that
\phi
belongs to
\mathrm{SS}\cap W_{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
. By
F2
we know that
\psi
is in
\mathrm{SS}
and it remains to show that
\psi
belongs to
W_{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
. This follows from the fact that
{\mathtt{ker}}(\phi)\cong{\mathtt{ker}}(\psi)
.
Since
\phi
is in
W_{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
we have

K\!K^{G}(D,{\mathtt{ker}}(\psi))=K\!K^{G}(D,{\mathtt{ker}}(\phi))=0
for all
D
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
. Consequently,
\psi
belongs to
W_{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
.
∎

For the formulation of universal properties it turned out to be useful to reformulate various exactness properties of functors defined on
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras using squares so that they make sense not only for stable targets, but also for pointed ones. But we will show in Lemma
2.14
below that these new definitions are equivalent to the old same-named
conditions in the situations where they have been used above.
We do these considerations at this point since we will use squares already in the proof of Proposition
2.15
below.

We consider a cartesian square

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces C}
\textstyle{D}

(2.27)

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
. We will consider the following two conditions on the
square in (
2.27
).

Definition 2.11
.

1.

The square (
2.27
) is called semisplit if there exist equivariant cpc’s
s\colon C\to A
and
t\colon D\to B
such that
p\circ s={\mathtt{id}}_{C}
and
q\circ t={\mathtt{id}}_{D}
.

2.

The square (
2.27
) is called split if there exist equivariant morphisms
s\colon C\to A
and
t\colon D\to B
such that
p\circ s={\mathtt{id}}_{C}
and
q\circ t={\mathtt{id}}_{D}
.

Remark 2.12
.

The conditions on
q
appearing in Definition
2.11
imply the corresponding conditions on
p
, see the verification of the axiom F2 in the proof of Proposition
2.10
for a proof of this fact.

∎

Note that an exact sequence

0\to I\to A\xrightarrow{q}B\to 0

(2.28)

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})

is semisplit or split, if and only if the cartesian square

\textstyle{I\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tilde{B}}

(2.29)

has the corresponding property as defined in Definition
2.11
.

Let
F\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to{\mathcal{D}}
(or
F\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\to{\mathcal{D}}
) be a functor to a pointed
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{D}}
. Corresponding to the list of conditions in Definition
2.11
we introduce the following two exactness conditions on the functor
F
.

Definition 2.13
.

1.

F
is semiexact if it is reduced and sends any semisplit cartesian square to a cartesian square in
{\mathcal{D}}
.

2.

F is split exact if it is reduced and sends any split cartesian square to a cartesian square in
{\mathcal{D}}
.

Let
F
be a functor as in Definition
2.13
.

Lemma 2.14
.

1.

If
{\mathcal{D}}
is stable, then
F
is semiexact in the sense of Definition
2.13
.
1
if and only if
F
sends all semisplit exact sequences to fibre sequences.

2.

If
{\mathcal{D}}
is additive, then
F
is split exact in the sense of Definition
2.13
.
2
if and only if

F
sends all split exact sequences to fibre sequences.

Proof.

In each case, the ”only if“ implication is obvious. To see the ”if“ implication, we extend the square (
2.27
) to the left by adding a pullback of
p
along
0\to C
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}

The left and the outer squares are exact sequences which are send by
F
to fibre sequences.
Thus applying
F
, in both cases
we obtain the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(I)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(D)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(p)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(q)}

(2.30)

in which the left and the outer squares are cartesian and the lower left corner is a zero object. In particular we can conclude that
F
is reduced. The right square is cartesian since
F(I)
identifies with the fibre of both
F(p)
and
F(q)
. The stable case follows at once, and in the additive case we use the existence of the splits of
F(p)
and
F(q)
to conclude.

∎

The following proposition shows all assertions of Theorem
1.3
except the one about triangulated structures which will be obtained later in Proposition
2.18
.

Proposition 2.15
.

1.

The functor
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is semiexact.

2.

The functor
\mathrm{ho}_{0}\colon\mathrm{ho}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G})\to\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
is an equivalence.

3.

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is stable.

Proof.

Assertion
1
is an immediate consequence of the fact that
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is the
\infty
-category associated to a category of fibrant objects whose fibrations are the semisplit exact surjections. Indeed, consider a semisplit cartesian square (
2.27
). Since
p
and
q
are fibrations this square represents a cartesian square in the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
, see
[
Cis19
, Prop. 7.5.6]
.

We now prove Assertion
2
.
We will use that forming localizations is compatible with going over to homotopy categories.
Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be an
\infty
-category with a set of morphisms
W
. Then
have a commutative square

\textstyle{{\mathcal{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{C}}[W^{-1}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{ho}({\mathcal{C}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ho}(\ell)}
\textstyle{\mathrm{ho}({\mathcal{C}}[W^{-1}])}

(2.31)

where
\mathrm{ho}(\ell)\colon\mathrm{ho}({\mathcal{C}})\to\mathrm{ho}({\mathcal{C}}[W^{-1}])
presents its target as the localization of
\mathrm{ho}({\mathcal{C}})
at the set
\mathrm{ho}(W)
in the sense of ordinary categories.
We apply this to
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
in place of
{\mathcal{C}}
. Since
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
is an ordinary category the left vertical functor in (
2.31
) is an equivalence. Hence
the functor

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\to\mathrm{ho}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G})

presents its target as the localization of

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
at the set of
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
-equivalences in the sense of ordinary categories. By Proposition

2.1
the functor

\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}

has the same universal property.
This implies that
\mathrm{ho}_{0}\colon\mathrm{ho}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G})\to\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
is an equivalence.

The proof Assertion
3
that
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is stable can now be copied from
[
LN18
, Prop. 3.3]
. First we note that
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
, being the
\infty
-category associated to a category of fibrant objects with a zero object, is pointed and has finite limits.
It remains to show that the loop functor
\Omega
in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is an equivalence. It is well-known that it suffices to show that
\Omega
induces an equivalence on the homotopy category, see for instance
[
LN18
, Lem. 3.4]
.

Using the explicit description of the fibrant replacement of
0\to A
given in Remark
2.9
we see that

\textstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(S(A))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A)}

(2.32)

is a pull-back in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
. Therefore
\Omega
is equivalent to
the suspension functor given by the dotted arrow
S
in (
2.17
).
In view of Assertion
2
shown above the induced action of this functor
S
on the homotopy category can be identified with the action of the functor
S_{0}
in Lemma
2.6
.
2
on
\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
. As asserted in the same statement
S_{0}
is an equivalence. ∎

Our next task is the verification of the properties of
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
and
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
stated in Theorem
1.4
.
Assertion
1.4
.
2
is already shown as a part of Proposition
2.15
.

The next Lemma settles Assertions
1.4
.
1
,
1.4
.
4
and
1.4
.
5
together.

Lemma 2.16
.

The functor
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is reduced,
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable, and homotopy invariant.

Proof.

We know that

\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
is reduced,
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable and homotopy invariant. Therefore
Proposition
2.15
.
2
together with the fact that the canonical functor from an
\infty
-category to its homotopy category detects equivalences and zero elements implies the assertion.
∎

The next lemma collects, for later reference, some simple consequences of the details proof of Proposition
2.15
.

Lemma 2.17
.

1.

Every morphism in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is equivalent to a morphism
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(f)
for a fibration
f
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
.

2.

Every product in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is equivalent to the image under
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
of a product in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
.

3.

Every fibre sequence in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is equivalent to the image under
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
of a cone sequence.

Proof.

These assertions follow from general facts about the associated
\infty
-category of a category of fibrant objects, where for Assertion
3
we use in addition that
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is stable.
For completeness we give alternative arguments which are specific to the present situation as we will use some of the details later.

Assertion
1
follows from the
equivalence
\mathrm{ho}_{0}\colon\mathrm{ho}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}

and the surjectivity of the arrow marked by
!!
in (
).

For Assertion
2
we note that any
object in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is equivalent to an object of the form
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A)
for some
A
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
. Let
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(B)
be a second object.
Then the square

\textstyle{A\oplus B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
