\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

is a split cartesian square in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
(Definition
2.11
.
2
).
Since

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is semiexact by Prop.
2.15
1
(and hence split exact)
we conclude that the square

\textstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A\oplus B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

(2.33)

is cartesian.
This implies that

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A)\times\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(B)\simeq\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A\oplus B)\,.

(2.34)

The assertion now follows from

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A\times B)\simeq\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A\oplus B)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{ergverwfwerferf}}}{{\simeq}}\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A)\times\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(B)\,,

where we use that the product of
C^{*}
-algebras is given by the sum.

We finally show Assertion
3
. Since
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is stable, a morphism in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}

can be extended to a fibre sequence, and every fibre sequence is obtained in this way up to equivalence. By Assertion
1
it suffices to consider fibre sequences obtained by extending the morphism
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(p)
for a morphism
p:A\to B
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
.
We next argue that one can further assume that
p
is a fibration. To this end we use
the cylinder defined in (
2.23
) in order to construct the commutative square

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\scriptstyle{e}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Cyl}(p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{B}

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
, where
q(a,b)=b(1)
and
e(a)=(a,{\mathtt{const}}_{p(a)})
. The morphism
e
is a
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
-equivalence since it is an instance of the morphism
u
in (
2.20
). Therefore
the two morphisms
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(p)
and
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(q)
are equivalent.

Note that
q
is surjective and that the exact cone sequence

0\to C(p)\to\mathrm{Cyl}(p)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle q}}{{\to}}B\to 0

(2.35)

is semisplit. Since
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is semiexact by Proposition
2.15
.
1
, it sends this sequence to a fibre sequence. This finishes the proof of Assertion
3
. ∎

As a homotopy category of a stable
\infty
-category, the
category
\mathrm{ho}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G})
acquires a triangulated structure

[
Lur
, Thm. 1.1.2.14]
. On the other hand
\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
has a triangulated structure
described in
[
MN06
, Sec. 2.1]
.

Proposition 2.18
.

The functor

\mathrm{ho}_{0}\colon\mathrm{ho}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G})\to\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
is an equivalence of triangulated categories.

Proof.

We know from Proposition
2.15
.
2
that
\mathrm{ho}_{0}
is an equivalence of categories.
Since sums in
\mathrm{ho}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G})
and
\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
are represented by sums in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
we conclude further
that
\mathrm{ho}_{0}
preserves sums and is therefore compatible with the
{\mathbf{Ab}}
-enrichment.

The inverse shift functor for
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
and therefore on
\mathrm{ho}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G})
is implemented by the suspension functor (
2.15
) on the level of
C^{*}
-algebras via Lemma
2.6
.
2
.
Similarly, by the description given in
[
MN06
, Sec. 2.1]
the inverse shift functor on
\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}

is also implemented by (
2.15
) via Lemma
2.6
.
2
.
This shows that
\mathrm{ho}_{0}
commutes with the shift functor.

On the one hand, by
[
MN06
, Sec. 2.1]
the exact triangles in
\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
are generated by the mapping cone sequences for morphisms in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
.
On the other hand, by Lemma
2.17
.
3

the fibre sequences in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
, and hence the exact triangles in
\mathrm{ho}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G})

are also generated by the mapping cone sequences for morphisms in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
.
We conclude that the functor
\mathrm{ho}_{0}
is compatible with the collections of distinguished triangles.
∎

This finishes the proof of Theorem
1.3
.

\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is stable by Proposition
2.15
.
2
and hence admits all finite colimits. The following
proposition strengthens this from finite to countable colimits and settles the remaining Assertions
1.4
.
5
and
1.4
.
6
.
For the notion of an admissible diagram
{\rm Nature}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
we refer to
[
MN06
, Def. 2.5]
. We will not repeat the definition here since the exact details are not relevant. We will only use
its consequence
[
MN06
, Prop. 2.6]
.

Lemma 2.19
.

1.

The category
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
admits all countable colimits and is therefore
idempotent complete.

2.

The functor
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
sends countable sums in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
to coproducts.

3.

The functor
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
preserves colimits of all diagrams
A\colon{\rm Nature}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
which are admissible.

Proof.

Since
{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}
is stable, in order to show that
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
admits all countable colimits it suffices to show that
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
admits countable coproducts. The
functor
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is essentially surjective by construction. Hence it suffices to show that for every countable family
(A_{i})_{i\in I}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
the family

(\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A_{i}))_{i\in I}
in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
admits a coproduct.
We consider the sum
A\coloneqq\bigoplus_{i\in I}A_{i}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})

and let
e_{i}\colon A_{i}\to A
be the canonical inclusion for every
i
in
I
.
Then we claim that

(\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A),(\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(e_{i}))_{i\in I})
represents the coproduct of the family
(\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A_{i})_{i\in I})
. In general, coproducts in a stable
\infty
-category can be detected on the homotopy category. In view of the equivalence
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}\simeq\mathrm{ho}\circ\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
given by (
1.2
) it thus suffices to show that

(\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}(A),(\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}(e_{i}))_{i\in I})
represents the coproduct in

\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
. But this follows from
[
Kas88
, Thm. 2.9]
stating that the family of maps
(e_{i})_{i\in I}
induces an isomorphism

\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}(A,B)\xrightarrow{\cong}\prod_{i\in I}\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}(A_{i},B)

for every
B
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
.
The proof of Assertion
1
is finished with the general observation that a stable and countably cocomplete
\infty
-category is idempotent complete.

The claim also implies Assertion
2
.

We finally show the Assertion
3
.
Let
A\colon{\rm Nature}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
be an admissible diagram.
We must show that the canonical map
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\rm Nature}}\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A)\to\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\rm Nature}}A)
is an equivalence.
To this end it suffices to show that the map

\mathrm{ho}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\rm Nature}}\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A){)}\to\mathrm{ho}(\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\rm Nature}}A))
obtained by composing with
\mathrm{ho}
from (
1.2
) is an isomorphism.
Using the isomorphism

\mathrm{ho}(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\rm Nature}}\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A))\cong{\mathtt{hocolim}}_{{\rm Nature}}\mathrm{ho}(\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A))\cong{\mathtt{hocolim}}_{{\rm Nature}}\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}(A)

and Proposition
2.18
(to translate homotopy colimits formed in the homotopy category of the stable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
to homotopy colimits formed in the triangulated category
\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
used in
[
MN06
]
), this is exactly the assertion of
[
MN06
, Prop. 2.6]
.
∎

The existence of coproducts in
\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
and the analogue of Assertion
2.19
.
2
for
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
has previously been shown in
[
MN06
, Prop. 2.1]
.

The proof of Theorem
1.4
is now complete.

In the following we consider the minimal and the maximal tensor products
\otimes_{\min}
and
\otimes_{\max}
of
C^{*}
-algebras. Both equip
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
with a symmetric monoidal structure.
Recall that a symmetric monoidal structure on a stable
\infty
-category is called bi-exact if the tensor product preserves cofibre sequences, and hence finite colimits, in each variable.
In this case the
\infty
-category together with its symmetric monoidal structure is called stably symmetric monoidal.

Proposition 2.20
.

The tensor product
\otimes_{?}
for
?
in
\{\min,\max\}
descends to a bi-exact symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
and
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
refines to a symmetric monoidal functor

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G,\otimes_{?}}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})^{\otimes_{?}}\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G,\otimes_{?}}\,.

Moreover, the tensor structure
\otimes_{?}
on
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
preserves countable colimits in each variable.

Proof.

In order to show that the tensor product descends along the localization

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}

such that
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
refines to a symmetric monoidal functor
we use
[
Hin16
, Prop. 3.2.2]
. By this result, it suffices to show that for every
A
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
the functor

A\otimes_{?}-
preserves
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
-equivalences, where
?
is in
\{\min,\max\}
.
It is easy to see that the functor

\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}\circ(A\otimes_{?}-):{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
preserves zero objects and is

\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable and split exact since
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}

has these properties. We now apply Corollary
2.4
in order to conclude that this composition sends
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
-equivalences to isomorphisms.
Hence
A\otimes_{?}-
preserves
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
-equivalences.

In order to show that the resulting symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is exact
we must show that
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A)\otimes_{?}-
in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
preserves fibre sequences for every
A
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
.
We will use the observation made in the proof of
Proposition
2.18
that every fibre sequence in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is equivalent to a cone sequence.

It suffices to show that

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A\otimes_{?}-)
sends cone sequences to fibre sequences.

For the maximal tensor product, using the exactness of
A\otimes_{\max}-
(see e.g.
[
BO08
, Prop. 3.7.1]
) and
the explicit description of the cone sequences in Remark
2.9

one checks that
A\otimes_{\max}-
preserves cone sequences in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
.
We then use that the functor
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
sends cone sequences to fibre sequences.

For the minimal tensor product we use the fact
that cone sequences admit completely positive contractive splits and that the
minimal tensor product is functorial for
completely positive contractive maps.
We conclude that
A\otimes_{\min}-
sends cone sequences to semisplit exact sequences.
We now use that
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is semiexact.

Since we already know that
\otimes_{?}
on
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is bi-exact, in order to show that the corresponding tensor structure on
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
preserves countable colimits in each argument it suffices to show that it preserves countable sums. As observed in the proof of Lemma
2.19
, countable sums in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
are presented by countable sums in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
. It follows from
Lemma
7.13
and the fact that the tensor products of
C^{*}
-algebras preserves finite sums, that

\otimes_{?}
on
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
preserves countable sums in each argument. Since
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
preserves countable sums by Lemma
2.19
.
2
we can conclude that
\otimes_{?}
on
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
preserves
countable sums, too.

∎

Remark 2.21
.

Alternatively, the argument in the proof of Proposition
1.7
given for the minimal tensor product also applies to the maximal tensor product
since the latter is also functorial for completely positive contractive maps
[
Pis
, Cor. 4.18]
.

∎

Remark 2.22
.

As a consequence of Proposition
2.20
the homotopy category

\mathrm{ho}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G})
and therefore
\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
has two tensor triangulated structures
induced by
\otimes_{\min}
and
\otimes_{\max}
, respectively. For
\otimes_{\min}
this fact is well known, see e.g.
[
MN06
, Sec. 2.5]
. It seems that the symmetric monoidal structure coming from the maximal tensor product has not been studied so much, but for the non-equivariant case see e.g.
[
LN18
, Lem. 3.13]
.

∎

Note that
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is, by Definition
1.2
, the initial functor from
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
to
\infty
-categories which sends
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
-equivalences to equivalences. In
Theorem
1.5
we stated a different universal property which better reflects the standard properties of
K\!K
-theory. We will now first state an intermediate
Theorem
2.23
involving additive
\infty
-categories in order to formulate a direct
\infty
-categorial analog of
[
Mey00
, Thm. 6.6]
which appeared in the present paper as Proposition
2.2
.

Recall from
[
Lur
, 6.1.6.13]
that an
\infty
-category is called semi-additive if it is pointed, admits finite products and coproducts, and if the canonical morphism
from a coproduct to the product of any two objects is an equivalence. The homotopy category of a semi-additive category is canonically enriched in abelian monoids. If all these morphism monoids are abelian groups then the
\infty
-category is said to be additive. Stable
\infty
-categories are additive.
In particular,
since
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is stable by Proposition
2.15
, it is additive.

Theorem 2.23
.

The functor
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is initial among functors
from
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
to objects of
{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{add}}_{\infty}
which are reduced,

\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable, and split exact.

Proof.

For an additive
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{D}}
we consider the full subcategory

{\mathbf{Fun}}^{{rse}}({\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}),{\mathcal{D}})

(2.36)

of
{\mathbf{Fun}}({\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}),{\mathcal{D}})
on functors which are reduced,
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable, and split exact.
By Corollary
2.4
the functor category (
2.36
)
is a full subcategory of the category

{\mathbf{Fun}}^{{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}}({\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}),{\mathcal{D}})

of functors sending
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
-equivalences to equivalences.

We now build the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\coprod}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G},{\mathcal{D}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Fun}}^{{rse}}({\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}),{\mathcal{D}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G},{\mathcal{D}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Fun}}^{{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}}({\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}),{\mathcal{D}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

(2.37)

The vertical morphisms are inclusions of full subcategories
and the horizontal functors are induced by precomposition with
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
.
The superscript
\coprod
stands for coproduct preserving functors which are the morphisms in
{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{add}}_{\infty}
. By the universal property of

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
as a Dwyer-Kan localization
the lower horizontal functor is an equivalence.
We now justify that the dashed arrow exists, making the diagram commute.
For this, we note that the localisation functor
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is reduced,
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable and semiexact by Theorem
1.4
. In particular it is split-exact. Thus if
H\colon\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\to{\mathcal{D}}
preserves coproducts, then the composition
H\circ\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is reduced,
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable and split exact.

In order to prove Theorem
2.23
we must show
that the dashed arrow in (
2.37
) is an equivalence. As all other functors in the diagram
2.37
are fully faithful, so is the dashed one. It remains to show that it is essentially surjective.

To this end we consider

F
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}^{{rse}}({\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}),{\mathcal{D}})
. In view of the lower horizontal equivalence in (
2.37
) there exists a
functor
\bar{F}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G},{\mathcal{D}})
and an equivalence
\bar{F}\circ\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\simeq F
.
We must check that
\bar{F}\in{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\coprod}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G},{\mathcal{D}})
, i.e., that
\bar{F}
preserves coproducts.

First of all
F
preserves the empty coproduct since
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
and
F
are reduced.

We now show that
\bar{F}
preserves binary coproducts. Since
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
and
{\mathcal{D}}
are additive, in both
\infty
-categories coproducts and products coincide.
Therefore it suffices to show that
\bar{F}
preserves binary products.
Since
F
is split-exact and reduced, it sends a split exact square of the form

\textstyle{A\oplus B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

to a cartesian square with zero in the lower right corner.
We conclude that
F
preserves products.
Now by Proposition
2.17
.
2
any product in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is equivalent to the image of a product in

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
. In view of the equivalence
\bar{F}\circ\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\simeq F
we can conclude that
\bar{F}
preserves binary products.

Hence
\bar{F}
preserves binary coproducts.
Since
F
preserves empty and binary coproducts it preserves all finite coproducts.
∎

Theorem
2.23
says that if
{\mathcal{D}}
is an object of
{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{add}}_{\infty}
and
F\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to{\mathcal{D}}
is a reduced,
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable, and split exact functor,
then there exists an essentially unique, finite coproduct-preserving factorization
\bar{F}
as indicated
in the diagram

\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{D}}\,.}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bar{F}}

Since
{\mathcal{D}}
and
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
are additive, in both
\infty
-categories products and coproducts coincide. Hence
\bar{F}
also preserves all finite products.
If
{\mathcal{D}}
admits finite limits, e.g. if
{\mathcal{D}}
is stable, one might wonder whether the functor
\bar{F}
in addition preserves finite limits. In general it does not, see Remark
2.25
for an example, but we have the following characterization.

Let
{\mathcal{D}}
be an additive
\infty
-category, and let

F\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to{\mathcal{D}}
be a functor which is reduced,
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable and split exact.
Let
\bar{F}\colon\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\to{\mathcal{D}}
be the factorization as explained above.
In the following statement we use the Definition
2.13
.
1
of semiexactness for functors with additive targets.

Theorem 2.24
.

If in addition
{\mathcal{D}}
admits finite limits and
F
is semiexact,
then
\bar{F}
preserves finite limits.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that
\bar{F}
is reduced and sends fibre sequences to fibre sequences. We already know that
\bar{F}
is reduced
by Theorem
2.23
.

By Proposition
2.17
.
3
, any fibre sequence in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is the image under
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
of a semisplit exact sequence in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
.
By assumption,
F
sends such a semisplit exact sequence to a fibre sequence. In view of the relation
\bar{F}\circ\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\simeq F
we conclude that
\bar{F}
sends the image of this semisplit exact sequence under
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
to a fibre sequence.
Consequently
\bar{F}
preserves fibre sequences.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.5
.

The theorem immediately follows by specializing the Theorems
2.23
and
2.24
to stable target categories.
∎

Remark 2.25
.

A natural example of a functor
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\to\mathbf{Sp}
which is reduced,

\mathbb{K}
-stable and split exact is the composite

C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Rings}_{\mathrm{inv}}^{\mathrm{nu}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{Sp}

where the first functor is the forgetful functor taking the underlying ring with involution of a
C^{*}
-algebra and the second takes the (projective, symmetric) L-theory spectrum of a ring with involution. This functor descends to a functor
{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}}\to\mathbf{Sp}
which preserves finite products, but is not exact. Indeed, the failure of exactness in this case can be described explicitly, we refer to
[
LN18
, Thm. 4.2]
for a general treatment.

∎

3
The s-finitary extension

Let
F\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\to{\mathcal{D}}
be a functor to a target category
{\mathcal{D}}
admitting all small filtered colimits.
Then we have a canonical natural transformation
\hat{F}\to F
, where
\hat{F}
is
the left Kan extension of
F_{s}\coloneqq F_{|{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})}
as indicated in the following diagram:

\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F_{s}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{incl}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Downarrow\ }
\scriptstyle{\hat{F}}
\scriptstyle{\>\Downarrow}
\scriptstyle{F\hskip 8.19447pt}

Lemma 3.1
.

The functor
F
is s-finitary if and only if the natural transformation
\hat{F}\to F
is an equivalence.

Proof.

The pointwise formula for the left Kan-extension
shows that

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{(A^{\prime}\to A)\in(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})_{/A}}F(A^{\prime})\simeq\hat{F}(A)\,.

If we compare this formula with the morphism (
1.14
) appearing in the condition for being s-finitary it becomes clear that
we must show that the poset of separable subalgebras of
A
is cofinal in
(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})_{/A}
. This follows from the following observation.

If
f\colon A^{\prime}\to A
is any morphism in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
, then we have a factorization

A^{\prime}\to f(A^{\prime})\to A\,,

where the image
f(A^{\prime})
is a
G
-
C^{*}
-subalgebra of
A
. If
A^{\prime}
is separable, then
f(A^{\prime})
is separable, too.
∎

The next lemma is the essential step for the derivation of Theorem
1.18
from Theorem
1.4
.
Let
F
and
F_{s}
be as above and assume in addition that
{\mathcal{D}}
is stable.

Lemma 3.2
.

Assume that
F
is s-finitary.

1.

If
F_{s}
is reduced, then so is
F
.

2.

If
F_{s}
is homotopy invariant, then so is
F
.

3.

If
F_{s}
is
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable, then so is
F
.

4.

If
F_{s}
is semiexact, then so is
F
.

5.

If
F_{s}
sends exact sequences to fibre sequences, then so does
F
.

Proof.

Assertion
1
is obvious since the zero algebra is separable.

We show Assertion
2
.
For every
t
in
[0,1]
let
{\mathtt{ev}}_{t}:C([0,1])\to{\mathbb{C}}
be the evaluation at
t
.
If
B
is an invariant separable subalgebra of
C([0,1])\otimes A
, then the values
({\mathtt{ev}}_{t}\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{A})(b)
for all
b
in
B
and
t
in
[0,1]
generate an invariant separable subalgebra
A^{\prime}
of
A
such that
B\subseteq C([0,1])\otimes A^{\prime}
.
Hence the invariant subalgebras of
C([0,1])\otimes A
the form
C([0,1])\otimes A^{\prime}
with
A^{\prime}
an invariant separable subalgebra of
A
is cofinal in all invariant separable subalgebras of
C([0,1])\otimes A
. Since we assume that
F
is s-finitary
we have the chain of equivalences

F(A)\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{A^{\prime}{\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}}F_{s}(A^{\prime})\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{A^{\prime}{\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}}F_{s}(C([0,1])\otimes A^{\prime})\simeq F(C([0,1])\otimes A)\,,

where the colimit runs over the poset of all invariant separable subalgebras
A^{\prime}
of
A
and
the middle equivalence is a consequence
of the assumption on
F_{s}
. Since this morphism is induced by the canonical map (
1.5
)
this
shows that
F
is homotopy invariant

We show Assertion
3
.
Let
A
be in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
,
let
H\to H^{\prime}
be an equivariant isometric inclusion of separable
G
-Hilbert spaces such that
H\not=0
, and
K(H)\to K(H^{\prime})
be the corresponding inclusion of the algebras of compact operators.
We note that these algebras are separable. It follows that the family of
G
-invariant subalgebras
A^{\prime}\otimes K(H)
for all
G
-invariant separable subalgebras
A^{\prime}
of
A
is cofinal in all separable
G
-invariant subalgebras of
A\otimes K(H)
, and similar for
H^{\prime}
.
Using that
F
is s-finitary we conclude that the morphism obtained by applying
F
to (
1.4
) has a factorization over the chain of equivalences

\displaystyle F(A\otimes K(H))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{A^{\prime}}F_{s}(A^{\prime}\otimes K(H))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{A^{\prime}}F_{s}(A^{\prime}\otimes K(H^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle F(A\otimes K(H))\,,

where the colimit runs over the poset of all invariant separable subalgebras
A^{\prime}
of
A

and the middle equivalence follows from the assumption on
F_{s}
.

We show Assertion
4
. We now consider a semisplit exact sequence

0\to A^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i}}{{\to}}A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\to}}A^{\prime\prime}\to 0

(3.1)

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
.
Let
s:A^{\prime\prime}\to A
denote the equivariant cpc split of
\pi
.
We consider the
family
{\mathcal{S}}
of exact sequences

{\mathcal{C}}\quad:\quad 0\to C^{\prime}\to C\to C^{\prime\prime}\to 0

of separable
G
-invariant subalgebras of (
3.1
) such that
s(C^{\prime\prime})\subseteq C
.
Such a sequence is again equivariantly semisplit since we can use the restriction of
s
. Since
F
is s-finitary
it is enough to show that
the families of constituents
C^{\prime}
,
C
, and
C^{\prime\prime}
for
{\mathcal{C}}
running in
{\mathcal{S}}
are cofinal families of
G
-invariant separable
C^{*}
-algebras in
A^{\prime}
,
A
and
A^{\prime\prime}
, respectively.
Indeed, then using the assumption on
F_{s}
the fibre sequence

F(A^{\prime})\to F(A)\to F(A^{\prime\prime})\to\Sigma F(A^{\prime})

is the colimit over
{\mathcal{S}}
of the family of fibre sequences

F_{s}(C^{\prime})\to F_{s}(C)\to F_{s}(C^{\prime\prime})\to\Sigma F_{s}(C^{\prime})\,.

Let
B^{\prime}
,
B
and
B^{\prime\prime}
be separable
C^{*}
-subalgebras of
A^{\prime}
,
A
, and
A^{\prime\prime}
, respectively.

Then the invariant
C^{*}
-subalgebra
C^{\prime\prime}:=A^{\prime\prime}(\pi(B),B^{\prime\prime})
of
A^{\prime\prime}
generated by the subsets
\pi(B)
and
B^{\prime\prime}
is separable and contains
B^{\prime\prime}
.
Then
C:=A(B,s(C^{\prime\prime}),i(B^{\prime}))
is a separable subalgebra of
A
containing
B
such that the restriction of
\pi
induces a surjection

C\to C^{\prime\prime}
and
s(C^{\prime\prime})\subseteq C
.
We finally define
C^{\prime}:={\mathtt{ker}}(C\to C^{\prime\prime})
considered as a subalgebra of
A^{\prime}
. Then

B^{\prime}\subseteq C
.
Since
B^{\prime}
is a closed subspace of the separable

C^{*}
-algebra it is again separable.

By construction we have an equivariantly semisplit exact sequence

0\to C^{\prime}\to C\to C^{\prime\prime}\to 0

in
{\mathcal{S}}

and

B^{\prime}\subseteq C^{\prime}\,,\quad B\subseteq C\,,\quad B^{\prime\prime}\subseteq C^{\prime\prime}\,.

The proof of Assertion
5
is again a small modification of of the proof
of Assertion
4
. Since we do not have a split at all, we define

C:=A(B,G\hat{C}^{\prime\prime},i(B^{\prime}))
, where
\hat{C}^{\prime\prime}
is a set of choices of preimages of a countable dense subset of
C^{\prime\prime}
.
Otherwise the argument is the same.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.18
.

Since the functor
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
is equivalent by Definition
1.10
to the left Kan-extension of its restriction
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
to
separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras
it is s-finitary by Lemma
3.1
.
This is Assertion
1.18
.
1
which verifies the assumption
in Lemma
3.2
for
F=\mathrm{kk}^{G}
and
F_{s}=\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
.

The remaining assertions of Theorem
1.18
now follow by applying Lemma
3.2

and Theorem
1.4
asserting the corresponding properties for
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
.
∎

Our next theorem settles the universal property of
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
stated as Theorem
1.19
in the introduction.

Theorem 3.3
.

The functor
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
is initial among functors from
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
to objects of
\mathbf{CAT}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{ccpl}\cap{\mathrm{ex}}}

which are s-finitary, reduced,
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable and semiexact.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{D}}
be a cocomplete stable

\infty
-category.
We then have the following chain of equivalences

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}}(\mathrm{KK}^{G},{\mathcal{D}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle y^{G,*},\simeq}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\mathrm{ex}}(\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G},{\mathcal{D}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G})^{*},\simeq}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Fun}}^{rse}({\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}),{\mathcal{D}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{incl}^{*},\simeq}}{{\leftarrow}}

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Fun}}^{frse}({\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}),{\mathcal{D}})\,,

where the superscript
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}
stands for colimit preserving functors. Furthermore the superscript
{(f)rse}
stands the full subcategory of functors which are (s-finitary,) reduced,
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable and semiexact, respectively.

3
3

3

Note that in the proof of Theorem
2.23
, the
e
in the superscript
rse
referred to split exact as opposed to semiexact in the present situation. We apologise for the notational clash.
.

Here for the first two steps we use the universal properties of
y^{G}
in (
1.6
) and
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
(Theorem
1.5
).
The last equivalence is a consequence of the Lemmas
3.1
and
3.2
which imply that
if we left Kan-extend a functor in

{\mathbf{Fun}}^{{rse}}({\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}),{\mathcal{D}})
along the inclusion
\mathrm{incl}
in (
1.7
),
then the result is in

{\mathbf{Fun}}^{{frse}}({\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}}),{\mathcal{D}})
.
Since
\mathrm{kk}^{G}\circ\mathrm{incl}\simeq y^{G}\circ\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
by (
1.7
)
we conclude

{\mathbf{Fun}}^{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}}(\mathrm{KK}^{G},{\mathcal{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbf{Fun}}^{frse}({\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}),{\mathcal{D}})

which is the desired equivalence.
∎

Remark 3.4
.

Note that the functor
y^{G}\colon\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}
in (
1.6
) only preserves finite colimits.
Therefore, in contrast to Proposition
2.19
.
2
, the functor
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
only sends finite sums to coproducts.
One could improve this situation by forming the Bousfield localization

L:\mathrm{KK}^{G}\rightleftarrows\mathrm{KK}^{G,\prime}:\mathrm{incl}

of
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
at the set of maps
\bigoplus_{n\in{\rm Nature}}\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A_{n})\to\mathrm{kk}^{G}(\bigoplus_{n\in{\rm Nature}}A_{n})
for all families

(A_{n})_{n\in{\rm Nature}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
and setting

\mathrm{kk}^{G,\prime}:=L\circ\mathrm{kk}^{G}:{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\to\mathrm{KK}^{G,\prime}\ .

We restrict to families of separable algebras in order have a set of generators.

The functor
\mathrm{kk}^{G,\prime}
is
s-finitary. In addition, it preserves countable sums. Indeed, it preserves countable sums of
families of separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras by construction. But using that
\mathrm{kk}^{G,\prime}
is s-finitary
we can remove the assumption on separability.

As an immediate consequence of Theorem
3.3
and the definitions the universal property of
\mathrm{kk}^{G,\prime}
is as follows: The functor

\mathrm{kk}^{G,\prime}
is initial among functors from
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
to objects of
\mathbf{CAT}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{ccpl}\cap\mathrm{ex}}

which are s-finitary, reduced,
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable, semiexact, and
which send countable sums to coproducts.
∎

The next proposition is Proposition
1.20
from the introduction.

Proposition 3.5
.

If
A
is in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
and
B
is in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\sigma})
,
then the functor
\mathrm{ho}
induces an isomorphism of
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded groups

\pi_{*}\mathrm{KK}^{G}(A,B)\cong K\!K^{G}_{*}(A,B)\,.

(3.2)

Proof.

Let
A
be in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
. We consider the functors

\pi_{*}\mathrm{KK}^{G}(A,-),\,K\!K^{G}_{*}(A,-)\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\sigma})\to{\mathbf{Ab}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}}\,.

(3.3)

By the compatibility of
\mathrm{ho}
with the triangulated structure stated in Theorem
1.3
.
2

it induces a natural isomorphism

\pi_{*}\mathrm{KK}^{G}(A,(-)_{s})\xrightarrow{\cong}K\!K^{G}_{*}(A,(-)_{s})

between the restriction of these functors to
separable
G
-
C^{*}
-algebras.
It therefore suffices to show that both functors in (
3.3
) are s-finitary.
Since
\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A)\simeq y^{G}(\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A))
is a compact object of
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
and
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
is
s
-finitary, the functor
\mathrm{KK}^{G}(A,-)
is s-finitary, too. Since
\pi_{*}
preserves filtered colimits also

\pi_{*}\mathrm{KK}^{G}(A,-)
is s-finitary.

In order to see that
K\!K^{G}_{*}(A,-)
is s-finitary, using the shift equivalence of the triangulated structure on
\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}
, it suffices to show this for
*=0
. We now use the formula
[
Mey00
, Thm. 5.5]
4
4

4

Alternatively, we could use
[
Ska85
, Rem. 3.2]
.
(at this point we need the assumption that
B
is
\sigma
-unital)
stating that

K\!K^{G}(A,B)\cong[q_{s}(A)\otimes K,B\otimes K]\,.

Here
K\coloneqq K(\ell^{2}\otimes L^{2}(G))
,
q_{s}(A)\coloneqq{\mathtt{ker}}(A\otimes K\sqcup A\otimes K\to A\otimes K)
, and
[-,-]
stands for taking homotopy classes of morphisms in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
.
We note that
K
is separable and
q_{s}(A)
is separable.
Every equivariant homomorphism
q_{s}(A)\otimes K\to B\otimes K

and every homotopy between such homomorphisms factorizes over an invariant separable subalgebra of the target. Furthermore, the separable subalgebras of the form
B^{\prime}\otimes K
for invariant separable subalgebras
B^{\prime}
of
B
are cofinal.
Consequently,
K\!K^{G}(A,-)
is s-finitary, too.
∎

We now turn to the proof of Proposition
1.21
from the introduction (repeated here as Proposition
3.8
) concerning symmetric monoidal structures on
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
and
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
.
As a preparation we show how left Kan extensions interact with symmetric monoidal structures.
Let
r\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}
be a symmetric monoidal functor between small symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories, and let
{\mathcal{D}}
be a presentably symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category.

Lemma 3.6
.

The left Kan extension
r_{!}F:{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{D}}
of a lax symmetric monoidal functor
F\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
along
r
has a naturally induced lax symmetric monoidal refinement.

Proof.

The restriction functor

r^{*}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{D}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})
is lax symmetric monoidal (with respect to the Day convolution structures on the functor categories). Furthermore it has a symmetric monoidal left-adjoint

r_{!}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{D}})
which is a symmetric monoidal refinement of the left Kan-extension functor, see
[
Nik
, Cor. 3.8]
.
This implies that
r_{!}
preserves commutative algebras.

By
[
Gla16
, Prop. 2.16]
,
[
Lur
, Sec. 2.2.6]

commutative algebras in these functor categories correspond to lax symmetric monoidal functors.
Therefore, if
F\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{D}}
is lax symmetric monoidal, it is a commutative algebra in
{\mathbf{Fun}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{D}})
. Consequently,
r_{!}F
is a commutative algebra in
{\mathbf{Fun}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{D}})
, and hence
r_{!}F
is lax symmetric monoidal.
∎

Remark 3.7
.

In proof of Proposition
3.8
below
we will use that the
\mathrm{Ind}
-completion
\mathrm{Ind}({\mathcal{D}})
of a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{D}}
admits a canonical symmetric monoidal structure preserving filtered colimits in each argument
such that the canonical functor
{\mathcal{D}}\to\mathrm{Ind}({\mathcal{D}})
has a canonical refinement
to a symmetric monoidal functor. If
{\mathcal{D}}
is stable and the monoidal structure on
{\mathcal{D}}
is exact, then the induced structure on
\mathrm{Ind}({\mathcal{D}})
is presentably symmetric monoidal, and the canonical functor is symmetric monoidal. A reference for these statements is
[
Lur
, Cor. 4.8.1.14]
.

∎

Proposition 3.8
.

The symmetric monoidal structure
\otimes_{?}
on
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
for
?
in
\{\min,\max\}
induces a
presentably symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
and
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
refines to a symmetric monoidal functor

\mathrm{kk}^{G,{\otimes}_{?}}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\otimes_{?}}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G,{\otimes}_{?}}\,.

(3.4)

Proof.

Applying Remark
3.7
to
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
with the symmetric monoidal structure
\otimes_{?}
for
?
in
\{\min,\max\}
constructed in Proposition
1.7
we get a presentably symmetric monoidal structure
\otimes_{?}
on
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
and a symmetric monoidal refinement

y^{G,\otimes_{?}}\colon\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G,\otimes_{?}}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G,{\otimes}_{?}}

of the functor in (
1.6
).
Since
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
has a symmetric monoidal structure by Proposition
1.7
,
the upper horizontal composition in the diagram

\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{incl}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{y^{G}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{G}}

has a symmetric monoidal structure, too.
Applying Lemma
3.6
to the symmetric monoidal functor
\mathrm{incl}
in place of
r
we conclude that

\mathrm{kk}^{G}
acquires a canonical lax symmetric monoidal refinement (
3.4
).

To show that
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
is in fact symmetric monoidal, we have to show that for

A
and
B
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
the canonical map

\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A)\otimes_{{?}}\mathrm{kk}^{G}(B)\to\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A\otimes_{?}B)

(3.5)

is an equivalence. Furthermore, we must show that the
unit morphism

1^{{\otimes_{?}}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}\to\mathrm{kk}^{G}({\mathbb{C}})

(3.6)

is an equivalence,
where
1^{\otimes_{?}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}
is the tensor unit for the structure
\otimes_{?}
on
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
, and
{\mathbb{C}}
is the tensor unit for
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
for both structures.

We start with the discussion of the unit morphism. By Remark
3.7
we have an equivalence
1^{\otimes_{?}}_{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}\simeq y^{G}(1_{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}})
.
By Proposition
1.7
we also know that

1_{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}\simeq\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}({\mathbb{C}})
. If we now apply
y^{G}
to the second equivalence
and compose with the first, then we get the desired equivalence (
3.6
).

We now give separate arguments for (
3.5
) in the cases where
?=\max
and
?=\min
.

We first consider the case of
\otimes_{\min}
. We will employ the following fact: If
A
and
B
are in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}

and
A^{\prime}
is a closed subalgebra of
A
and
B^{\prime}
is a closed subalgebra of
B^{\prime}
, then the canonical map

A^{\prime}\otimes_{\min}B^{\prime}\to A\otimes_{\min}B
is an isometric inclusion
[
BO08
, Prop. 3.6.1]
.

The desired equivalence (
3.5
) for
\otimes_{\min}
is given by the following chain of equivalences:

\displaystyle\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A)\otimes_{\min}\mathrm{kk}^{G}(B)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}\limits_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}A,B^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}B}\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A^{\prime})\otimes_{\min}\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(B^{\prime})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}\limits_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}A,B^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}B}\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A^{\prime}\otimes_{\min}B^{\prime})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(!)}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A\otimes_{\min}B)\,.

In the first step, we have used that
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
is s-finitary and that the tensor product in
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
commutes with colimits in each variable. In the second step we use that
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is symmetric monoidal for
\otimes_{\min}
by Proposition
1.7
. To justify the final equivalence
(!)
, it suffices to show that the poset of subalgebras of
A\otimes_{\min}B
of the form
A^{\prime}\otimes_{\min}B^{\prime}
(note that here we use the fact from above), for separable subalgebras
A^{\prime}
and
B^{\prime}
of
A
and
B
, respectively, is cofinal in the poset of all separable subalgebras. To see this, let
C
be an invariant separable subalgebra of
A\otimes_{\min}B
. Let
(c_{i})_{i\in I}
be a countable dense subset of
C
.
For every
i
in
I
we can choose a countable family of elementary tensors

(a_{i,j}\otimes b_{i,j})_{j\in J_{i}}
such that
c_{i}
belongs to the closure of the linear span of this family. We then let
A^{\prime}
be the subalgebra generated by the
G
-orbits of the elements
a_{i,j}
for all
i
in
I
and
j
in
J_{i}
.
It is an invariant separable subalgebra of
A
. We define
B^{\prime}
similarly using the elements
b_{i,j}
.
By construction we have
C\subseteq A^{\prime}\otimes_{\min}B^{\prime}
.
This finishes the argument in the case of
\otimes_{\min}
.

We now consider the maximal tensor product
\otimes_{\max}
. The problem here is that if
A^{\prime}
and
B^{\prime}
are as above then in general the canonical map
A^{\prime}\otimes_{\max}B^{\prime}\to A\otimes_{\max}B
is not an isometric inclusion. Denoting the image of this map by

A^{\prime}\bar{\otimes}_{\max}B^{\prime}
we get the surjection

A^{\prime}\otimes_{\max}B^{\prime}\to A^{\prime}\bar{\otimes}_{\max}B^{\prime}
.

We use that
A\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}A}A^{\prime}
,
B\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{B^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}B}B^{\prime}
and the fact that the maximal tensor product commutes with filtered colimits (see Lemma
7.13
.
1
) in order to conclude that

A\otimes_{\max}B\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}A,B^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}B}A^{\prime}\otimes_{\max}B^{\prime}\,,

(3.7)

where as before the colimit runs over the poset of
(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})
of pairs of invariant separable subalgebras of
A
and
B
, respectively.
By a similar cofinality argument as in the case of the minimal tensor product we also get

A\otimes_{\max}B\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}A,B^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}B}A^{\prime}\bar{\otimes}_{\max}B^{\prime}\,.

(3.8)

In the following we will show the claim that
the inductive systems
(A^{\prime}\otimes_{\max}B^{\prime})_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}A,B^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}B}
and

(A^{\prime}\bar{\otimes}_{\max}B^{\prime})_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}A,B^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}B}
are isomorphic in
\mathrm{Ind}(C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
.

For the moment we fix
A^{\prime}
and
B^{\prime}
. We then get the outer part of the following diagram

\textstyle{A^{\prime}\otimes_{\max}B^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}\bar{\otimes}_{\max}B^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\prime\prime}\otimes_{\max}B^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\otimes_{\max}B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\otimes_{\max}B}

The vertical morphisms are the canonical inclusions into the colimits which have been identified using (
3.7
) and (
3.8
).

Since
A^{\prime}\otimes_{\max}B^{\prime}
is separable, the kernel
I
of
A^{\prime}\otimes_{\max}B^{\prime}\to A^{\prime}\bar{\otimes}_{\max}B^{\prime}
is separable. By Example
7.17

the poset of separable subalgebras in a
C^{*}
-algebra is countably filtered
5
5

5

This means that every countable subset admits an upper bound.
.
Since
G
is countable, using Lemma
7.18
we can find invariant separable subalgebras
A^{\prime\prime}
and
B^{\prime\prime}
containing
A^{\prime}
and
B^{\prime}
, respectively, such that
I
is annihilated by the map

A^{\prime}\otimes_{\max}B^{\prime}\to A^{\prime\prime}\otimes_{\max}B^{\prime\prime}
. This provides the dotted morphism. The existence of
(A^{\prime\prime},B^{\prime\prime})
and the dotted arrow for any given
(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})
proves the claim.

The claim justifies the equivalence marked by
(!)
in the following chain of equivalences

\displaystyle\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A)\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{kk}^{G}(B)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}\limits_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}A,B^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}B}\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A^{\prime})\otimes_{\max}\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(B^{\prime})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}

\displaystyle\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}\limits_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}A,B^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}B}\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A^{\prime}\otimes_{\max}B^{\prime})

\displaystyle{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(!)}}{{\simeq}}}

\displaystyle\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}\limits_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}A,B^{\prime}\subseteq_{{\mathrm{sep}}}B}\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A^{\prime}\bar{\otimes}_{\max}B^{\prime})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{{\simeq}}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{kk}^{G}(A\otimes_{\max}B)

providing the equivalence (
3.5
) in the case of the maximal tensor product.
The remaining equivalences in this chain are justified in the same way as in the case of the minimal tensor product.
∎

4
Change of groups functors

In this section we show that the restriction, induction and crossed product functors of
C^{*}
-algebras
descend to functors between the corresponding universal KK-theoretic stable
\infty
-categories. The following lemma is the blue-print for
the assertions about the change of groups functors below.

Let
G
and
H
be countable groups and consider a functor

C\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\,.

If
A\to B
is a morphism in

{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
, then we let
{C(A)}^{C(B)}
denote the image of the induced morphism
C(A)\to C(B)
. If
C
preserves separable algebras and
A
is separable, then
C(A)^{C(B)}
is separable, too.

For any category
{\mathcal{C}}
let
\mathrm{Ind}({\mathcal{C}})
denote the category of inductive systems in
{\mathcal{C}}
.
For
A
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
we consider the inductive system

(A^{\prime})_{A^{\prime}{\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}}
of the invariant separable subalgebras
A^{\prime}
of
A
in
\mathrm{Ind}({\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}))
.
We then have a canonical map of inductive systems

(C(A^{\prime}))_{A^{\prime}{\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}}\to(C(A^{\prime})^{C(A)})_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}
in
\mathrm{Ind}({\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}))
.

Assume that
C
preserves separable algebras.

Definition 4.1
.

We say that
C
is
\mathrm{Ind}
-s-finitary if it has the following properties:

1.

For every
A
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
the inductive system
(C(A^{\prime})^{C(A)})_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}
is cofinal in the inductive system of all invariant separable
subalgebras of
C(A)
.

2.

The canonical map
(C(A^{\prime}))_{A^{\prime}{\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}}\to(C(A^{\prime})^{C(A)})_{A^{\prime}{\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}}
is an isomorphism in

\mathrm{Ind}({\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}))
.

This definition is designed in order to ensure the following fact.

Lemma 4.2
.

If
F
is some
s
-finitary functor on
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
and
C
is
\mathrm{Ind}
-s-finitary, then the composition

F\circ C
is an
s
-finitary functor on
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
.

Proof.

For any
A
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
we must show that the canonical morphism

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}F(C(A^{\prime}))\to F(C(A))

(4.1)

is an equivalence. It first follows from Condition
4.1
.
2
that

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}F(C(A^{\prime}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}F(C(A^{\prime})^{C(A)})\,.

By Condition
4.1
.
1

we have an equivalence

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{A^{\prime}\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}F(C(A^{\prime})^{C(A)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{B^{\prime}\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}C(A)}F(B^{\prime})\,.

Finally, since
F
is
s
-finitary, we have an equivalence

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{B^{\prime}\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}C(A)}F(B^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}F(C(A))\,.

The composition of these equivalences is the desired equivalence (
4.1
).
∎

In order to check that a functor
C\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
is
\mathrm{Ind}
-s-finitary we will use the following lemma.

Lemma 4.3
.

Assume that
C
preserves separable algebras, satisfies the Condition
4.1
.
1
and one of the following:

1.

C
preserves countably filtered colimits.

2.

C
preserves isometric inclusions.
6
6

6

Note that inclusions of
C^{*}
-algebras are automatically isometric.

Then
C
is
\mathrm{Ind}
-s-finitary.

Proof.

We must show that each of the Assumptions

1
or
2
implies Condition
4.1
.
2
.

In the case of Assumption
2
we actually have isomorphisms

C(A^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}{C(A^{\prime})^{C(A)}}
for every invariant separable subalgebra
A^{\prime}
of
A
so that Condition
4.1
.
2
is clear.

We now consider the more complicated case of Assumption
1
.
The argument is very similar to the case of

\otimes_{\max}
in the corresponding part of the proof of Proposition
3.8
.
We consider an invariant separable subalgebra
A^{\prime}
of
A
giving the outer part of the following diagram

\textstyle{C(A^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{C(A^{\prime})^{C(A)}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C(A^{\prime\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C(A)}

(4.2)

Every
C^{*}
-algebra is isomorphic to the colimit of its separable subalgebras.
In the case of
A
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
for a
countable group
G
we have the same assertion for the system of invariant separable subalgebras
(A^{\prime})_{A^{\prime}{\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}}
, i.e.,
we have an isomorphism
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{A^{\prime}{\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}}A^{\prime}\cong A
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
.
The poset of invariant separable subalgebras of
A
is countably filtered (see Example
7.17
).
Since
C
preserves countably filtered colimits we have

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{A^{\prime}{\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}}C(A^{\prime})\cong C(A)
, and the left vertical arrow is the canonical inclusion into the colimit.
Let
I
be the kernel of the map

C(A^{\prime})\to{C(A^{\prime})^{C(A)}}
. Since
C(A^{\prime})
is separable, also
I
is separable. Furthermore it is contained in (in fact equal to) the kernel of
C(A^{\prime})\to C(A)
.
By Lemma
7.18

we find an invariant separable subalgebra
A^{\prime\prime}
of
A

such that
I
is annihilated by
C(A^{\prime})\to C(A^{\prime\prime})
.
This implies the existence of the dotted arrow.
The existence of
A^{\prime\prime}
for given
A^{\prime}
shows that
the canonical map of inductive systems

(C(A^{\prime}))_{A^{\prime}{\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}}\to({C(A^{\prime})^{C(A)}})_{A^{\prime}{\subseteq_{\mathrm{sep}}A}}
has an inverse in
\mathrm{Ind}({\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}))
.
∎

We set
C_{s}\coloneqq C_{|{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})}
.

Lemma 4.4
.

Assume:

1.

C
is
\mathrm{Ind}
-s-finitary.

2.

C_{s}
preserves countable sums.

3.

The composition
\mathrm{kk}^{H}_{\mathrm{sep}}\circ C_{s}
inverts
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
-equivalences.

4.

C_{s}
preserves semisplit exact sequences.

Then there are functors
F
and
F_{s}
taking part in the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F_{s}}
\scriptstyle{y^{G}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}
\scriptstyle{C_{s}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{incl}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{incl}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{H}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{H}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{y^{H}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{G}}
\scriptstyle{C}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{H}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{H}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}

(4.3)

In addition:

1.

F_{s}
is exact and preserves countable coproducts.

2.

F
preserves colimits and compact objects.

Both
F
and
F_{s}
are characterized by the diagram and these properties.

Proof.

The existence of the factorization
C_{s}
and the commutativity of the left square in (
4.3
) expresses the fact that
C
preserves separable algebras
as part of Assumption
1
.
The middle square is an instance of (
1.7
).

The existence of the factorization
F_{s}
follows from Assumption
3
and the defining universal property of
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
as a Dwyer–Kan localization.
Using Assumption
4
and the semiexactness of
\mathrm{kk}^{H}
we conclude that
\mathrm{kk}^{H}_{\mathrm{sep}}\circ C_{s}
is semiexact, too.
This implies by Theorem
2.24
that
F_{s}
is exact. To see that
F_{s}
preserves countable coproducts, consider a countable family
{(A_{i})_{i\in I}}
of objects of
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
.
As seen in the proof of Lemma
2.19

countable sums in
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
are presented by countable sums in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})
. Furthermore, by Lemma
2.19
.
2
,
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
sends countable sums to countable coproducts. This implies the equivalences marked by
(1)
in the chain

\displaystyle F_{s}(\bigoplus\limits_{i\in I}\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A_{i}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1)}}{{\simeq}}F_{s}(\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(\bigoplus\limits_{i\in I}A_{i}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2)}}{{\simeq}}\mathrm{kk}^{H}_{\mathrm{sep}}(C_{s}(\bigoplus\limits_{i\in I}A_{i}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(3)}}{{\simeq}}\mathrm{kk}^{H}_{\mathrm{sep}}(\bigoplus\limits_{i\in I}C_{s}(A_{i}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1)}}{{\simeq}}\bigoplus\limits_{i\in I}\mathrm{kk}^{H}_{\mathrm{sep}}(C_{s}(A_{i}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2)}}{{\simeq}}\bigoplus\limits_{i\in I}F_{s}(\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}(A_{i}))\ .

The equivalences marked by
(2)
are given by the upper triangle in (
4.3
).
In order to argue that
(3)
is an equivalence we use that
C_{s}
preserves countable sums of algebras. Using once more that
\mathrm{kk}^{H}_{\mathrm{sep}}
sends countable sums to countable coproducts, we obtain that
F_{s}
preserves countable coproducts.

It follows from Assumption
1
in combination with Lemma
4.2
and the fact that
\mathrm{kk}^{H}
is s-finitary that also
\mathrm{kk}^{H}\circ C
is s-finitary. Using the
existence of
F_{s}
together with Proposition
3.2
we then verify that
\mathrm{kk}^{H}\circ C
satisfies the assumptions of Theorem
3.3
. We therefore obtain the colimit-preserving factorization
F
from the
universal property of
\mathrm{kk}^{G}
.

We claim that then also the right square commutes, i.e., that
y^{H}\circ F_{s}\simeq F\circ y^{G}
. In order to see this we observe that the fillers of the other squares provide an equivalence
y^{H}\circ F_{s}\circ\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\simeq F\circ y^{G}\circ\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
. Then the desired equivalence is again a consequence of the defining universal property of
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
as a Dwyer–Kan localization.

The
compact objects in
\mathrm{KK}^{G}
are given by the essential image of
y^{G}
since
\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
is idempotent complete by the Theorem
1.4
.
5
. Therefore
the
commutativity of the right square implies that
F
also preserves compact objects.
∎

Remark 4.5
.

Note that
F
can alternatively be described as the functor
\mathrm{Ind}(F_{s})
obtained by the applying the
\mathrm{Ind}
-completion functor to
F_{s}
.

The fact that
F
preserves colimits and compact objects implies that

F
admits a right-adjoint which preserves filtered colimits. By stability
it hence preserves all colimits and therefore has a further right-adjoint.
Below we will describe these right-adjoints explicitly in some cases.

∎

Let
H\to G
be a homomorphism of countable groups.
The following lemma verifies Theorem
1.22
.
1
.

Lemma 4.6
.

We have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H,s}}
\scriptstyle{y^{G}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H,s}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{incl}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{incl}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{H}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{H}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{y^{H}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{G}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{H}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{H}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}}

(4.4)

where

1.

\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H,s}{\colon\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{H}}
is exact and preserves countable coproducts.

2.

\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}{\colon\mathrm{KK}^{G}\to\mathrm{KK}^{H}}
preserves colimits and compact objects.

Proof.

The assertions follow from Lemma
4.4
once we have verified its assumptions.
It is obvious that
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}
preserves separable algebras,
semisplit exact sequences and countable sums.
It is furthermore also obvious that the composition
{\mathrm{kk}^{H}_{\mathrm{sep}}}\circ\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H,s}
is reduced,

\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stable, and semiexact.
By the Theorem
1.5
it therefore inverts
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}}
-equivalences.

It remains to show that
C
is
\mathrm{Ind}
-s-finitary.
We first verify Condition
4.1
.
1
.
Let
A
be in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
and
B^{\prime}
be an
H
-invariant separable subalgebra of
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(A)
. Since
G
is countable the
G
-invariant subalgebra
A^{\prime}
generated by

B^{\prime}
is again separable and we have
B^{\prime}\subseteq\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(A^{\prime})
.
Condition
4.1
.
2
follows from Lemma
4.3
since
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}
preserves isometric inclusions.
∎

Remark 4.7
.

From now one we will use the same notation
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}
for all restriction functors
induced by
H\to G
and hope that this does not produce confusion as the argument of the functor determines which version has to be considered. The same convention will later also apply to the functors
\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}
and
-\rtimes_{{?}}H
considered below.

∎

The following corollary generalizes
[
MN06
, (12)]
from the triangulated to the
stable
\infty
-categorical level.

Corollary 4.8
.

The functor

\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}\colon\mathrm{KK}^{G}\to\mathrm{KK}^{H}
has a symmetric monoidal refinement for both symmetric monoidal structures
\otimes_{\min}
and
\otimes_{\max}
.

Proof.

We let
\otimes
denote one the symmetric monoidal structures
\otimes_{\min}
or
\otimes_{max}
.

We first show the claim that
the functor
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H,s}\colon\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{H}
has a symmetric monoidal refinement.
We consider the bold part of the diagram

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.67673pt}{-13.26309pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\ .

Assuming that
\mathrm{kk}^{H}_{\mathrm{sep}}\circ F
sends
\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{G}
-equivalences to equivalences we obtain the factorization
\bar{F}
indicated by the dashed arrow. Note that
\mathrm{kk}^{H}_{\mathrm{sep}}
and
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
have a symmetric monoidal refinements
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{H,\otimes}
and
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G,\otimes}
by Proposition
1.21
.
We now use

[
Hin16
, Prop. 3.2.2]
saying that

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G,\otimes}
has the universal property of a Dwyer-Kan localization
for symmetric monoidal functors which is compatible with the operation of
taking underlying functors.
Applying this to
the composition
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{H,\otimes}\circ F^{\otimes}
we get a symmetric monoidal refinement
\bar{F}^{\otimes}
of
\bar{F}
.
Applying this argument to
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H,s}
in place of
F
shows that claim.

The ind-completion functor
\mathrm{Ind}\colon{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{ex}}_{\infty}\to\mathrm{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}}
is a symmetric monoidal functor and hence induces a functor on commutative algebras. Interpreting the symmetric monoidal functor

\mathrm{Res}^{G,\otimes}_{H,s}:\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G,\otimes}\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{H,\otimes}
as a morphism of commutative algebras in
{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{ex}}_{\infty}
,
we get the desired symmetric monoidal refinement

\mathrm{Res}^{G,\otimes}_{H}:=\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Res}^{G,\otimes}_{H,s}):\mathrm{KK}^{G,\otimes}\to\mathrm{KK}^{H,\otimes}
of the functor
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}
.

∎

We now assume that
H
is a subgroup of
G
.
The following construction describes the induction functor
\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}
.

Construction 4.9
.

For
B
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
we let
C_{b}(G,B)
denote the
C^{*}
-algebra of bounded
B
-valued functions on
G
with the
\sup
-norm. The group
G
acts on
C_{b}(G,B)
by
(g,f)\mapsto(g^{\prime}\mapsto f(g^{-1}g^{\prime}))
.
We define the induction functor

\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})

as follows:

1.

objects: The induction functor
sends
B
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg})
to the invariant subalgebra
\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(B)
of
C_{b}(G,B)
generated by the functions

f\colon G\to B
satisfying

(a)

f(gh)=h^{-1}f(g)
for all
h
in
H
and
g
in
G
.

(b)

The
projection of
{\mathtt{supp}}(f)
to
G/H
is finite.

2.

morphisms: The induction functor sends a morphism
\phi\colon B\to B^{\prime}
to the morphism

\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(\phi)\colon\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(B)\to\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(B^{\prime})
given by

\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(\phi)(f)(g)\coloneqq\phi(f(g))
.

We have a natural transformation of functors

\iota\colon{\mathtt{id}}\to\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}\circ\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{H}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\,.

(4.5)

Its evaluation at
B
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
is given by the homomorphism

\iota_{B}\colon B\to\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{H}(B))

which sends
b
in
B
to the function

G\to B\ ,\quad g\mapsto\begin{cases}g^{-1}(b)&\text{ if }g\in H\,,\\
0&\text{ if }g\notin H\,,\end{cases}

on
G
.

∎

We frequently need the following well-known result.
Let
B
be in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
and
A
be in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
.

Lemma 4.10
.

For
?
in
\{\min,\max\}

we have a canonical isomorphism

\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(B)\otimes_{?}A\cong\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(B\otimes_{?}\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(A))\,.

(4.6)

Proof.

Let
\iota_{B}\colon\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(B)\to C_{b}(G,B)
denote the canonical inclusion. By construction of the induction functor we have the following commutative square
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})

\textstyle{C_{b}(G,B)\otimes_{?}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{b}(G,B\otimes_{?}A)\ }
\textstyle{\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(B)\otimes_{?}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{B}\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{A}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{H}(B\otimes_{?}\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(A))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{B\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{A}}}

In order to show that the lower horizontal map is an isomorphism
we choose a section
r
of the projection map
G\to G/H
and extend the diagram non-equivariantly as follows:

\textstyle{C_{b}(G,B)\otimes_{?}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r^{*}\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{A}}
\textstyle{C_{b}(G,B\otimes_{?}A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r^{*}}
\textstyle{C_{b}(G/H,B)\otimes_{?}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C_{b}(G/H,B\otimes_{?}A)}
\textstyle{C_{0}(G/H,B)\otimes_{?}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{!}
\textstyle{C_{0}(G/H,B\otimes_{?}A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(B)\otimes_{?}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{B}\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{H}(B\otimes_{?}\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(A))}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{B\otimes{\mathtt{id}}_{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

The lower vertical isomorphisms are
immediate consequences of the construction of the induction functor.
The arrow marked by
!
is an isomorphism
because of

C_{0}(G/H,B)\otimes_{?}A\cong C_{0}(G/H)\otimes_{?}B\otimes_{?}A\cong C_{0}(G/H,B\otimes_{?}A)\,.

Hence the lower horizontal morphism is an isomorphism, too. ∎

The following lemma verifies Theorem
1.22
.
2
.

Lemma 4.11
.

We have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{H}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{H,s}}
\scriptstyle{y^{H}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{H}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{H,s}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{incl}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{incl}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{H}_{\mathrm{sep}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{y^{G}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{H}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{H}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{kk}^{H}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{G}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{KK}^{H}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{H}}

(4.7)

where

1.

\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{H,s}{\colon\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{H}\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}
is exact and preserves countable coproducts.

2.

\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{H}{\colon\mathrm{KK}^{H}\to\mathrm{KK}^{G}}
preserves colimits and compact objects.

Proof.

The assertion will again follow from Lemma
4.4
.
In comparison with the case of
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}
the verification of Assumption
4.4
.
3

is considerably more complicated. It will employ non-formal results of Kasparov
[
Kas88
]
which we will use in the form stated in
[
MN06
]
(see Remark
4.12
).

It is easy to see that
\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{H}
preserves separable algebras and sums.
In order to see that it is
\mathrm{Ind}
-s-finitary we must check the Conditions
4.1
.
1
and
4.1
.
2
.
Note that
\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}
preserves isometric inclusions.
Let
A
be in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
and
B^{\prime}
be a separable
subalgebra of
\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(A)
. Then we let
A^{\prime}
be the
H
-invariant subalgebra of
A
generated by the values
f(g)
for all
f
in
B
and
g
in
G
(see Construction
4.9
for notation). Since
G
is countable
A^{\prime}
is separable. Furthermore
B^{\prime}\subseteq\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(A^{\prime})
. Finally,
Condition
4.1
.
2
follows from
Lemma
4.3
using again that
\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}
preserves isometric inclusions.

Next we show that
\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}
preserves semisplit exact sequences. To this end let
p\colon A\to B
be a surjective morphism in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
and
s\colon B\to A
be an equivariant completely positive contraction
such that
p\circ s={\mathtt{id}}_{B}
.
Then we define (using notation from Construction
4.9
) a map of vector spaces
\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(s)\colon\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(B)\to\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(A)

by
\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(s)(f)(g)\coloneqq s(f(g))
. This map preserves the generators (i.e., functions satisfying the Conditions
4.9
.
1a
and
4.9
.
1b
) by the linearity, continuity and equivariance of
s
.
We now show that
\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(s)
extends by continuity to a completely positive contraction.
We choose a section of the projection map
G\to G/H
. The restriction along this section
induces an isomorphism of
C^{*}
-algebras

\mathrm{Res}^{G}(\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(B))\cong C_{0}(G/H)\otimes_{\min}\mathrm{Res}^{H}(B)

and similarly for
A
. Under this identification the map
\mathrm{Res}^{G}(\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(s))
acts as

{\mathtt{id}}_{C_{0}(G/H)}\otimes s
. This map extends by continuity to a completely positive contraction.
For this last step we use that completely positive contractions are compatible with minimal tensor products
[
BO08
, Thm. 3.5.3]
.

We now show that the composition
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\circ\mathrm{Ind}_{H,s}^{G}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
inverts

{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{H}}
-equivalences.
We give a short argument using results from the literature.
Using the adjunction
(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H},\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{H})
on the level of
triangulated categories

[
MN06
, (20)]
and the Yoneda lemma we see that the composition

\mathrm{ho}\circ{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}\circ\mathrm{Ind}_{H,{s}}^{G}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{G}

sends
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{H}}
-equivalences to isomorphisms.
Since
\mathrm{ho}
detects equivalences we conclude that
{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}\circ\mathrm{Ind}_{H,{s}}^{G}

sends
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{H}}
-equivalences to equivalences.
∎

Remark 4.12
.

The details of the arguments leading to
[
MN06
, (20)]
are not very well documented in the literature.
7
7

7

The text before (9) in
[
MN06
]
suggests that the authors only consider
\mathbb{K}
-stability while one needs
\mathbb{K}^{G}
-stability in order to apply the universal property from Corollary
2.4
.

Therefore we sketch now an alternative argument for the fact that
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\circ\mathrm{Ind}_{H,s}^{G}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathrm{sep}})\to\mathrm{KK}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}
inverts

\mathrm{KK}_{0}^{H}
-equivalences
which is close to the general philosophy of the present paper. We consider the
G
-Hilbert spaces
V_{G}\coloneqq{L^{2}(G)}
and
V_{G}^{\prime}\coloneqq{\mathbb{C}}\oplus V_{G}
, where
{\mathbb{C}}
has the trivial
G
-action.
We define

\widetilde{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G}_{H}\colon{\mathbf{Fun}}(BH,{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}})

by

\widetilde{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G}_{H}(B)\coloneqq\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(B\otimes_{\min}\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}K(V_{G}))\,.

(4.8)

Since
K(V_{G})
is separable, the functor
\widetilde{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G}_{H}
preserves separable algebras. As before we indicate the restriction of an induction functor to separable algebras by a subscript ‘s’, but in order to simplify the notation we drop this subscript at restriction functors.
We claim:

1.

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\circ\widetilde{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G}_{H{,s}}
sends
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{H}}
-equivalences to equivalences.

2.

We have an equivalence

\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\circ\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{H{,s}}\simeq{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}\circ\widetilde{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G}_{H,s}
.

Both assertions together imply that
\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}\circ\mathrm{Ind}_{H,s}^{G}
inverts
{\mathrm{kk}_{0}^{H}}
-equivalences.

We first show Assertion
2
of the claim as follows.
We consider the diagram

B\to B\otimes_{\min}\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}K(V^{\prime}_{G})\leftarrow B\otimes_{\min}\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}K(V_{G})

where the maps are induced by the obvious
G
-equivariant isometric embeddings

{\mathbb{C}}\to V_{G}^{\prime}
and
V_{G}\to V^{\prime}_{G}
.
We now assume that
B
is separable, apply
{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}\circ\mathrm{Ind}_{H{,s}}^{G}
and use (
4.6
) in order to get

\displaystyle\mathclap{{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}(\mathrm{Ind}^{G}_{H{,s}}(B))\to{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}(\mathrm{Ind}_{H{,s}}^{G}(B)\otimes_{\min}K(V_{G}^{\prime}))\leftarrow{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}(\mathrm{Ind}_{H{,s}}^{G}(B)\otimes_{\min}K(V_{G}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{def.}}}{{=}}{\mathrm{kk}_{\mathrm{sep}}^{G}}(\widetilde{\mathrm{Ind}}_{H{,s}}^{G}(B))\,.}

By
