134.65622pt\raise-32.00891pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 134.65622pt\raise-32.00891pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A({\mathbf{D}}\otimes_{\min}{\mathbf{E}})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

As seen above the lower horizontal morphism is isometric. The
vertical morphisms are isometric by
[
Bun
, Lem. 6.7]
.
It follows that the upper horizontal morphism is isometric.
Since
C,C^{\prime}
and
E,E^{\prime}
were arbitrary this shows Assertion
2
for functors which are in addition injective on objects.

Since
-\otimes_{\min}{\mathbf{E}}
sends unitary equivalences to unitary equivalences and hence to isometries we can remove the
assumption that
\phi
is injective on objects using the same construction as in the proof of
[
BE
, Thm. 18.6]
.
∎

The following proposition partially generalizes Lemma
7.13
from
C^{*}
-algebras to
C^{*}
-categories.
Let
{\mathbf{I}}
be a small category and
{\mathbf{C}}\colon{\mathbf{I}}\to{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}}}
be a diagram.

Proposition 7.24
.

We assume that
{\mathbf{I}}
is filtered.

1.

If the structure maps of the diagram are injective on objects, then
the canonical morphism

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathbf{C}}\otimes_{\max}{\mathbf{D}})\to(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}{\mathbf{C}})\otimes_{\max}{\mathbf{D}}

(7.18)

is an isomorphism.

2.

If the structure maps of the diagram are faithful functors, then

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathbf{C}}\otimes_{\min}{\mathbf{D}})\to(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}{\mathbf{C}})\otimes_{\min}{\mathbf{D}}

(7.19)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We first consider the case of
\otimes_{\max}
.
We get

\displaystyle A({\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}\,({\mathbf{C}}\otimes_{{\max}}{\mathbf{D}}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lem.\penalty\ }\ref{wegoihjiogewrgergegrewf}}}{{\cong}}

\displaystyle{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}\,A({\mathbf{C}}\otimes_{{\max}}{\mathbf{D}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Prop.\penalty\ }\ref{eroigjweogieogigw}}}{{\cong}}

\displaystyle{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}\,(A({\mathbf{C}})\otimes_{{\max}}A({\mathbf{D}}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lem.\penalty\ }\ref{eriguhwiegugwergwerg}.\ref{weoigjweorrerggwergr}}}{{\cong}}

\displaystyle({\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}\,A({\mathbf{C}}))\otimes_{{\max}}A({\mathbf{D}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lem.\penalty\ }\ref{wegoihjiogewrgergegrewf}}}{{\cong}}

\displaystyle A({\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}\,{\mathbf{C}})\otimes_{{\max}}A({\mathbf{D}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Prop.\penalty\ }\ref{eroigjweogieogigw}}}{{\cong}}

\displaystyle A({\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}\,{\mathbf{C}}\otimes_{{\max}}{\mathbf{D}})\,.

We first observe that the functor in (
7.19
)
induces a bijection on the level of objects. We then use
that functor
A
detects isomorphisms among functors which are bijections on objects. Therefore
arguing as in the proof of Lemma
7.23
we remove
A
to conclude that (
7.18
)
is an isomorphism.
In the case of
\otimes_{\min}
we use that

-\otimes_{\min}{\mathbf{D}}
preserves faithful functors by Proposition
7.23
.
2

and that the images of
{\mathbf{C}}(i)\otimes_{\min}{\mathbf{D}}\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}{\mathbf{C}}\otimes_{\min}{\mathbf{D}}

for all
i
in
{\mathbf{I}}
together generate
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}{\mathbf{C}}\otimes_{\min}{\mathbf{D}}
.
We can therefore apply Proposition
7.22
.
∎

Let
{\mathbf{I}}
again be a small category,
G
be a group, and let
{\mathbf{C}}\colon{\mathbf{I}}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
be a diagram.

Proposition 7.25
.

We assume that
{\mathbf{I}}
is filtered.

1.

If the structure maps of the diagram are injective on objects, then
the canonical morphism

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathbf{C}}\rtimes_{\max}G)\to(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}{\mathbf{C}})\rtimes_{\max}G

(7.20)

is an isomorphism.

2.

If the structure maps of the diagram are faithful functors, then

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}({\mathbf{C}}\rtimes_{r}G)\to(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}{\mathbf{C}})\rtimes_{r}G

(7.21)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The proof is analoguous to the proof of Proposition
7.24
. We replace
-\otimes_{?}{\mathbf{D}}
by
-\rtimes_{?}G
.

For
?=\max
we use the compatibility of

A
with the maximal crossed product
[
Bun
, Thm 8.6]
.
Furthermore we use Lemma
4.15
.
1
instead of Lemma
7.13
.
1
.

In the case of
?=r
we want to apply Proposition
7.22
.
First note
that the reduced crossed product preserves
faithful functors
[
BE
, Thm. 12.23]
verifying Assumption
7.22
.
1
. In order to verify Assumption
7.22
.
2

note that the reduced crossed product
(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}{\mathbf{C}})\rtimes_{r}G

is generated by the image of the algebraic crossed product

(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}{\mathbf{C}})\rtimes^{\mathrm{alg}}G
. Since
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}{\mathbf{C}}
in turn is generated by the images of
C(i)
for all
i
in
{\mathbf{I}}
we can conclude that

(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}{\mathbf{C}})\rtimes_{r}G
is generated by the images of

C(i)\rtimes^{\mathrm{alg}}G
. Hence

(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{{\mathbf{I}}}{\mathbf{C}})\rtimes_{r}G
is in particular generated by the images of
C(i)\rtimes_{r}G
for all
i
in
{\mathbf{I}}
. This verifies Assumption
7.22
.
2
and Proposition
7.22
implies our assertion.
∎

Remark 7.26
.

We do not know whether in Propositions
7.24
.
1
and
7.25
.
1

the assumption that the structure maps of the diagram are injective on objects is really necessary.
∎

Appendix A
Applications to assembly maps

12
12

12

Hier muss gecheckt werden, ob alle suspensions korrekt sind.

We now introduce a
\mathrm{KK}
-valued version of the Davis–Lück assembly map by specializing the general constructions from
[
BE
, Sec. 19]
. We furthermore explain its relation with the classical assembly map appearing in the Baum–Connes conjecture, thereby previewing some of the results from
[
BEL
]
.

We start with
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})
and let
\ell
be the localization map from
(
6.15
). We let
j^{G}\colon BG\to G\mathbf{Orb}
denote the fully faithful inclusion of
BG
into the orbit category of
G
which sends
the unique object of
BG
to the orbit
G
. We then form the left Kan extension

j^{G}_{!}\ell({\mathbf{C}})
as indicated by the dotted arrow in the diagram

\textstyle{BG\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell({\mathbf{C}})}
\scriptstyle{j^{G}}
\textstyle{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}_{\infty}\,.}
\textstyle{G\mathbf{Orb}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{G}_{!}\ell({\mathbf{C}})}

We compose this left Kan extension with the functor
\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat},\infty}
from (
6.17
) (for the trivial group
G
) and obtain the functor

k^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\coloneqq\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat},\infty}(j^{G}_{!}\ell({\mathbf{C}}))\colon G\mathbf{Orb}\to\mathrm{KK}

(A.1)

which
is an instance of the functor in
[
BE
, Def. 19.3]
. By Elmendorf’s theorem this functor determines a

\mathrm{KK}
-valued equivariant homology theory

H(-,k^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}})\colon G{\mathbf{Top}}\to\mathrm{KK}\,,\quad X\mapsto H(X,k^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}})

(A.2)

on
G
-topological spaces.

We can calculate the values of the functor
k^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
in (
A.1
) explicitly. We consider a subgroup
H
of
G
.
By the point-wise formula for the left Kan extension, the equivalence

BH\simeq BG_{/(G/H)}
, by
[
Bun
, Thm. 7.8]
(expressing the colimit over
BH
in terms of the maximal crossed product), and using Proposition
6.16
we get the equivalences

\displaystyle k^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(G/H)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle{\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat},\infty}}(j^{G}_{!}\ell({\mathbf{C}})(G/H))

(A.3)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat},\infty}(\smash{\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{BH}}\ \ell({\mathbf{C}}))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}({\mathbf{C}}\rtimes_{\max}H)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{H}({\mathbf{C}})\rtimes_{\max}H\,,

where we omitted to write
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}
at various places.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be a family of subgroups of
G
and denote by
G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}
the full subcategory of
G\mathbf{Orb}
of
G
-orbits with stabilizers in
{\mathcal{F}}
.

Definition A.1
.

The Davis–Lück assembly map for the family
{\mathcal{F}}
and the functor
k^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
is defined by

\mathrm{Asmbl}_{{\mathcal{F}},k^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}}\colon\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{G_{{\mathcal{F}}}\mathbf{Orb}}k^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\to k^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(*)\,.

(A.4)

Expressed in terms of the homology theory
H(-,k^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}})
in (
A.2
) this map is equivalent to the map

\mathrm{Asmbl}_{{\mathcal{F}},k^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}}}\colon H(E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G,k^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}})\to H(*,k^{G}_{{\mathbf{C}}})

induced by the map
E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G\to*
, where
E_{{\mathcal{F}}}G
is a
G
-
CW
-complex representing the homotopy type of the classifying space of
G
for the family
{\mathcal{F}}
.

In the following we explain the relation of the assembly map (
A.4
) with the classical assembly map
appearing in the Baum–Connes conjecture
[
BCH94
]
. The details of this comparison will be developed in
[
BEL
]
.

The constructions in
[
BEL
]
depend on the choice of a
C^{*}
-category
{\mathbf{C}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
which admits small AV-sums
[
BE
, Def. 7.1]
.
We let
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat})
denote the invariant full subcategory of
{\mathbf{C}}
of unital objects
[
BE
, Def. 2.4]
. We furthermore consider the category
{\mathbf{C}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(BG,C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}^{\mathrm{nu}})
defined in
[
BEL
, Def. 2.15]
.

We use the definition

K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}\coloneqq\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat},\infty}(-))\colon C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}_{\infty}\to\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}

for the
K
-theory functor for
C^{*}
-categories. If we insert

k^{G}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}^{u}}
from (
A.1
) into
\mathrm{KK}({{\mathbb{C}}},-)
we get the functor

K^{G,\mathrm{top}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\coloneqq K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}(j_{!}^{{G}}\ell({{\mathbf{C}}}^{u}))\colon G\mathbf{Orb}\to\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}\,.

On the other hand, we can consider the functor

K^{G,{\mathrm{An}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\coloneqq\Sigma K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})}\colon G_{\mathbf{Fin}}\mathbf{Orb}\to\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}

(A.5)

obtained by specializing the coefficients of the functor in (
1.13
) at
\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}})
and applying the suspension functor
\Sigma
.

If
H
is a finite subgroup of
G
, then
we have an equivalence (we again omitted
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}
at various places)

\displaystyle K^{G,\mathrm{top}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}(G/H)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{def}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},k_{{{\mathbf{C}}}^{u}}^{G}(G/H))

(A.6)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{ergoijerwogwergergrewf}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{H}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{u})\rtimes_{\max}H)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ref{wtgijoogwrewegewgrg}.\ref{gjeriogjreerwgwregwerg}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{KK}^{H}({\mathbb{C}},{{\mathbf{C}}}^{u})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq_ind_res_adjunction_IC}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{KK}^{G}(C_{0}(G/H),{{\mathbf{C}}}^{u})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{def}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle K^{G,\mathrm{an}}_{\mathrm{kk}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}^{G}({{\mathbf{C}}}^{u})}(G/H)\,.

The right-hand side of (
A.6
) looks similar to the evaluation of (
A.5
) at
G/H
(up to a suspension), the difference lies in the coefficient categories

{{\mathbf{C}}}^{u}
and
{{\mathbf{C}}}^{(G)}_{\mathrm{std}}
, respectively.
But as a consequence of the general Paschke duality theorem which will be shown in
[
BEL
]
one can indeed show that these functors are equivalent. In the following we explain this in more detail.
The comparison involves a third functor

{K{\mathbf{C}}^{G}}\colon G\mathbf{Orb}\to\mathbf{Sp}^{\mathrm{}}

defined in
[
BEL
, Def. 13.2]
. Equivalently, this functor is given by
[
BE
, Def. 19.12]
if one sets

\mathrm{Hg}=K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}
and replaces (in the reference)
{\mathbf{C}}
by
{\mathbf{C}}^{u}
. The notation for the functors

K^{G,\mathrm{top}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}
and
{K{\mathbf{C}}}^{G}
which is used in
[
BE
]
is
{K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}}^{G}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}^{u},\max}
and

{K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}}^{G}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}^{u},r}
, respectively.
Because
K^{C^{*}\mathbf{Cat}}
is Morita invariant, by
[
BE
, Prop. 19.14.3]

we have an equivalence

(K^{G,\mathrm{top}}_{{\mathbf{C}}})_{|G_{\mathbf{Fin}}\mathbf{Orb}}\to({K{\mathbf{C}}}^{G})_{|G_{\mathbf{Fin}}\mathbf{Orb}}\,.

(A.7)

The following equivalence is the left vertical Paschke duality equivalence in
[
BEL
, (15.26)]
:

({{\Sigma}K{\mathbf{C}}}^{G})_{|G_{\mathbf{Fin}}\mathbf{Orb}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}K^{G,{\mathrm{An}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\,

(A.8)

(note that the r.h.s. is only defined on
G_{\mathbf{Fin}}\mathbf{Orb}
).
Combining (
A.8
)
and (
A.7
) we obtain the equivalence

({\Sigma}K^{G,\mathrm{top}}_{{\mathbf{C}}})_{|G_{\mathbf{Fin}}\mathbf{Orb}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}K^{G,\mathrm{An}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\,.

(A.9)

The relation of the assembly map
\mathrm{Asmbl}_{\mathbf{Fin},k^{G}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}^{u}}}
from (
A.4
) (note the superscript
u
) with the classical Baum–Connes assembly map
\mathrm{Asmbl}^{BC}
is now best explained by the following diagram:

where the colimit in the lower left corner runs over the
G
-finite subcomplexes of
E_{\mathbf{Fin}}G
.
The arrow marked by
(1)
is up to inserting definitions the canonical map

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{G_{\mathbf{Fin}}\mathbf{Orb}}\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},k^{G}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}^{u}})\to\mathrm{KK}({\mathbb{C}},\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{G_{\mathbf{Fin}}\mathbf{Orb}}k^{G}_{{{\mathbf{C}}}^{u}})\,.

Using stability of
\mathrm{KK}
it is an equivalence since
\mathrm{kk}({\mathbb{C}})
is a compact object of
\mathrm{KK}
. The upper square commutes by construction.
The arrows marked by
(2)
and
(3)
are induced by the natural transformation
c
from
[
BE
, Prop. 19.14.1]
. The corresponding square commutes by the
naturality of this transformation.
The map
(4)
is induced by the canonical map from the maximal to the reduced crossed product.
The equivalence marked by
(5)
is obtained by inserting the calculation (
A.3
) for
G=H

into the definitions.
The equivalence marked by
(6)
is justified by

[
BE
, Cor. 19.13]
. The square involving these two maps commutes by an inspection of the construction of the natural transformation
c
in
[
BE
, (19.19)]
.
The equivalence
(7)

is given by

[
BEL
, Prop. 14.5]
.
Finally,
the equivalence
(8)

involves the Paschke duality and is induced by the
left vertical equivalence in

[
BEL
, (15.26)]
.
The map
\mathrm{Asmbl}^{BC}
is defined in
[
BEL
, Def. 13.8]
.
On the level of homotopy groups it
is equal to the classical Baum–Connes assembly map

[
BCH94
]
, slightly extended to
G
-
C^{*}
-categories as coefficients.

The following is the second main result of
[
BEL
, Thm. 1.7]
:

Theorem A.2
.

The lower square in (
) commutes after taking homotopy groups.

The commutative diagram in (
)
implies the compatibility
of the Baum–Connes and the Davis–Lück assembly maps. This fact has been stated in
[
HP04
]
, and a complete proof has recently been given in

[
Kra
]
(see also
[
BEL
, Sec. 16]
for a detailed review)
using methods which are completely different from the ones in
[
BEL
]
.
